- **`window` below `min_samples`** is raised to `min_samples` with `orchestrator.tuning.window: must be at least min_samples (<n>) (using <n>)`, also when only `min_samples` was written (the default 200 then sits below it).
- **RH-5's explicit read.** `configured.s`/`.m` is true when `[orchestrator.budget.s]`/`.m` sets at least one of `tool_calls`, `minutes`, `tokens` (present, valid or not). An empty table is not explicit: the Settings screen deletes a value set back to its default but leaves the table's header (`settings/write.rs`, the `(Some(i), None)` arm). Tested in `an_explicit_budget_is_configured`.
- **Extra test:** `a_list_naming_only_skipped_entries_is_empty_and_bad_shapes_are_problems` (bad `pick`, non-array `candidates`, a non-table route).
- **Sizes.** `orchestrator.rs` 519→523 (+4), `orchestrator/unknown.rs` 120→125 (+5). New: `orchestrator/tuning.rs` 206, `orchestrator/routes.rs` 200, `orchestrator/tuning_tests.rs` 398.
- **Fix round 1 (rulings T7-1, T7-2, review minors).** This supersedes the first two bullets above. `config::{TuningConfig, Tuning, ConfiguredBudgets, RouteLists, RouteList, Candidate, Pick}` are now re-exported: `pub use routes::…` in `tuning.rs`, `pub use tuning::…` in `orchestrator.rs`, and the names added to `lib.rs`'s existing `pub use orchestrator::{…}`. `crates/config/tests/tuning_names.rs` names them from outside the crate. `Problem`'s `Display` (`lib.rs`) prints a `default` of `ENTRY_SKIPPED` (`"entry skipped"`) as `(entry skipped)`, so a dropped candidate reads exactly as decision 9a. `orchestrator.models[i]`'s dropped entries, which use the same default, now print that way too. A candidate's `model` that is not a string is `must be a string`, a missing one is `is required`, and an empty one is still `must not be empty`. The field carries a `///` doc line. New tests:
  - `a_bad_candidate_model_or_entry_says_what_is_wrong`;
  - three pinning tests: `bad_table_shapes_and_types_keep_defaults`, `the_u64_keys_have_upper_bounds`, and `min_samples_alone_above_the_default_window_raises_it`, which pins the window problem on an unwritten key as intentional.

  Sizes: `lib.rs` 540→549 (8 lines are the display skip form; the re-export reflows the list by 1), `orchestrator.rs` 519→525 (+6: decision 3's +4, plus the `pub use` and the doc line), `routes.rs` 202, `tuning.rs` 208, `tuning_tests.rs` 510, `tests/tuning_names.rs` 35.

### Task M9.5.8 (2026-10-03): refit, statistics, budgets, weights and proposals

Decisions 4–9 and 11 (pure half) and rulings RH-1 to RH-5 and RH-7 as written, with the controller addenda: the sample filter reads `history_io::effective_reverts`, and `a_revert_of_a_revert_makes_the_task_a_sample_again` pins that a task whose revert was reverted is a sample again (and out again after a third revert).

- **Files.** `run/refit.rs` came to 811 lines, so it is split by responsibility into three files, all pure (the Verification grep and the floating-point grep print nothing for each): `refit.rs` 444 (classes, ladders, samples, quality evidence, statistics, budgets, the ceiling, weights, `refit`), `refit_propose.rs` 227 (threshold and route proposals, `apply`, `dismiss`) and `refit_tuned.rs` 202 (`Tuned`, `tuned`, `report`), the last two child modules of `refit` (`#[path]`), whose public items `refit` re-exports, so every name of Interfaces is `run::refit::…`. `refit_render.rs` 101. Tests: `refit_tests.rs` 577, `refit_tests_quality.rs` 375 and a third file, `refit_tests_text.rs` 126 (the report and render tests, moved out to keep `refit_tests.rs` under 600). `stats.rs` 365→358 (`class` delegates to `refit::class_of`; `stats::median` stays, generic over the `u32` and `u64` columns, with the same lower-middle rule as `refit::lower_median`). `run/mod.rs` +2.
- **`ClassRoutes` is added here**, in `model_tuning.rs` (89→111), with Interfaces' `Default` (standard/low, standard/medium, frontier/high): `Tuned.routes` needs it. M9.5.9's file list names it; it only uses it now.
- **The fixture rules, where the brief is silent or contradicts itself.** (1) **M records are in runs `m1` and `m2`** (`m` + `1 + j / 10`, S's rule), not all in `m1`: with the default per-(run, round) cap of 10, twelve records of one run and round give 10 samples, and the CLI block would read `M      10/30`, against Interfaces' exact `M      12/30`. This keeps the cap and the block both as written; **it needs the controller's confirmation** (the alternative is to keep `m1` and change the block to `10/30`). (2) Every record has `sessions = 1` (the brief says so for S only; "every other field zero" would make M and hub records sessionless). (3) Hub records follow M's rule (`tool_calls = 60 + j`, `w = 1500`, `lines = 50 + j`, M's route) at `at = 1_790_150_000 + 60 × j`. (4) `quality.jsonl` numbers tasks within each run (`q<1 + i / 10>/t<i % 10>`, so the bisect culprit `t3` of `q2` names a task), `at = 1_790_300_000 + 60 × i`, 20 tool calls, 400 working seconds, 10 lines. The fixtures were written once by a throwaway `#[ignore]` test from `fixture_records`, and `fixtures_match_their_rules` regenerates and compares them byte for byte (and through `read_history`).
- **A configured class is refitted into `tuning.toml` too**; `tuned` and `report` decide its use (ruling RH-5's precedence). That is what lets the run log's start line say `configured (refit would be 55 calls 18m)` from the file alone, and `report`'s `refit_budget` hold the refit "used or not". Its refit-write line is logged like any class's.
- **`RefitState`**: `Off` when `refit_budgets = false`, else `Configured` when explicit, else `Written { at }` when the file has the class's budget, else `Kept` when the class qualifies (a refit within `min_change_percent` was not written), else `NotYet`. `refit_budget` is the file's refit (while the refit is on), so `Kept` has none: nothing was written.
- **Additions beyond Interfaces**, for M9.5.9's `ladder_budget::ceiling` and `resolve_task`: `refit::ceiling(class, effective_m, budget_l) -> Budget` (RH-4; the token axis is the larger of L's and twice M's only when both have one, else none), `Tuned::effective(cfg, class) -> Budget` (decision 6's precedence below the plan's `[task.budget]`), `SizeClass::ALL`, and `refit::budget_text` (shared with `refit_render`). `tuned` also writes decision 12's start lines into `Tuned.log` (`budget …`, `path weights …`, `thresholds …`, `route … (applied)`, else `none (…)`); M9.5.9 tests them, M9.5.8 tests the two budget shapes.
- **Small readings.** `failed_on_quality` counts a run-level revert in effect (`task_id: None`) against every task of that run, as decision 33's "or, with `task_id = None`, its run" does for samples. The M threshold is dropped when not above the *current* S threshold (the file's, else 20), not a proposed one. A class route outside its ladder (only a hand edit can make one) proposes nothing. `TuningChange`'s `class` is the file key (`"s"`, `"m"`). `TuningReport.orchestrator_list` is the first candidate as `<runtime>/<model>[ <effort>]`. A class list's candidate without `effort` is printed with the class's effort.
- **Concern: a moved bad file's line has no error text.** Interfaces' line is `tuning: tuning.toml did not parse (<error>); it was moved to <path> …`, but `TuningReport` carries only `moved_bad_file: Option<PathBuf>`, so `render` prints `tuning: tuning.toml did not parse; it was moved to <path> and tuning starts again from history`. Printing the error needs a `#[serde(default)]` field on `TuningReport` (protocol 16 already), for M9.5.9 or a ruling.
- **Tests beyond the list:** `class_of_moved_from_stats_unchanged`, `a_revert_of_a_revert_makes_the_task_a_sample_again` (addendum), `render_says_when_the_budget_refit_is_off`.
- **Fix round 1 (rulings T8-5, T9-1, review minors).** The token refit reads only budget samples that recorded worker usage (`worker_usage` not all zero); it refits a class's tokens only when that subset has `min_samples` and a lower median above 0, else `tokens` stays `None` and the configured token budget (or none) holds. `moved` is false when `new == cur`, so 0 → 0 is no change and nothing is rewritten at every start. A change in `PathWeights.derived` rewrites the weights. A configured class's refit is written on any change (it is only shown, so it has no 20 % gate): `run stats`, Settings and the start line show `refit would be` whenever history has one. `refit::lower_median` calls `stats::median` (now `pub(super)`). Quality evidence (`refit::Quality`: reverts in effect, bisect culprits, fixed stages) is built once per `proposals` pass; `failed_on_quality` builds it for one record. `refit::ceiling` holds T9-1's token rule (M or hub: the larger of L's and twice M's; M without tokens leaves L's; L without tokens leaves none), and `engine/ladder_budget.rs::ceiling` calls it with no fallback of its own (135→125). Tests in the new `refit_tests_edges.rs` (189): `the_token_refit_reads_only_samples_with_recorded_usage` (half recorded → `None`, no rewrite loop, a configured token budget kept), `a_zero_median_of_recorded_usage_refits_no_tokens`, `zero_to_zero_is_no_change`, `a_change_of_what_is_derived_rewrites_the_weights`, `a_configured_class_shows_a_refit_below_the_change_gate`, `rung_4_token_ceiling_falls_back_per_axis`. Sizes: `refit.rs` 444→496, `refit_propose.rs` 227→229, `refit_tuned.rs` 203→196.
- **Fix round 2 (rulings T8-6, T8-7).** This supersedes fix round 1's "a configured class's refit is written on any change" and the T8-4 note above. **T8-7:** `refit` neither refits nor writes a configured class: its `[budgets.<class>]` in `tuning.toml` is left exactly as it is, and removing the configuration later goes through the normal path from history and the normal 20 % gate. What it would be is computed for display only by the new `refit::shown_refit(lines, cfg, class)` (no change gate): `report`'s `refit_budget` for a configured class, and the start line of the new `refit::tuned_with(lines, file, cfg)`, which `driver/tuning.rs::tune_blocking` now calls. `refit::tuned(file, cfg)` stays, as `tuned_with(&[], …)`: with no history it cannot know a refit, so a configured S or M says only `tuning: budget S 30 calls 10m configured` (hub only with a refit); task 9's `plan_tests_tuning.rs` assertion is updated to that line. An exact-equal refit still shows (accepted). **T8-6:** `refit::ceiling(class, own, effective_m, budget_l)` gains `own`, the class's own effective budget, and never returns below it on any axis (a token axis with no ceiling stays without one; `own` with no tokens adds none); `ladder_budget::ceiling` passes `budget_s`, `budget_m`, or `budget_hub` else `budget_m`. The defaults are unchanged (S 150/60, M and hub 300/120). **Nit:** `lower_median` takes `&[u64]`. Tests: `the_ceiling_never_drops_below_the_classs_own_budget`, `a_configured_class_is_never_written_to_the_file` (`refit_tests_edges.rs`, 310) and the engine test `an_s_refit_above_m_keeps_its_own_ceiling` (`engine/tests/tuning.rs`, 155→172); `a_configured_class_shows_a_refit_below_the_change_gate` and `a_configured_budget_beats_the_refit` now assert nothing is written. Sizes: `refit.rs` 516, `refit_tuned.rs` 216, `ladder_budget.rs` 131.
- **Fix round 3 (ruling T8-8, review N5).** With nothing learned from history (no refit used or shown, no weights, thresholds or applied routes), `tuned_with` still writes decision 12's `tuning: none (history has fewer than <min_samples> samples per class)`, after any bare `… configured` lines (`nothing_learned_still_says_none_after_the_configured_lines`). `daemon/tests/tuning_io.rs::a_configured_class_start_line_shows_the_history_refit` drives `tune_for_start` with `configured.s` and the `refit.jsonl` history and asserts the `configured (refit would be 55 calls 18m)` start line and no S budget in the file; reverting `tune_blocking`'s call to `refit::tuned(&used, cfg)` fails it. `refit_tuned.rs` 221.

### Task M9.5.9 (2026-10-03): the tuning file, a run's start, and the thresholds in prompts

Decisions 6 (precedence and ceiling), 10, 12 and 13 and rulings RH-4, RH-5 and RH-8 as written, with the controller addenda: ruling T8-2's `TuningReport.parse_error` (`#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]`, protocol stays 16; round-trip and old-MessagePack test `a_tuning_report_without_its_parse_error_still_decodes`), and `refit_render` prints the moved file's line as decision 10 (`(<error>)`) through a shared `refit_render::moved_bad_line`, which the start's run log uses too.

- **`tune_for_start(config, locks, repo_dir, now)`** takes the start's `&config::Orchestrator`, not `&RunContext`: `build_delivered` already holds its one read of the settings (`RunContext.settings`' rule), and the budgets `build_run` freezes must come from the same config the tuning was computed with. It is called once, in `build_delivered`, with `repo_dir = profile::repo_dir(data_dir, pre.project)` (what `choose_profile` and the run's `repo_dir` use). An empty `repo_dir` (history off) returns the untuned value: config's lists and explicit budgets, no refit, no log line, no file touched.
- **The lock.** `TuningLocks` (in `RunService`, `driver.rs` +3) maps each repository data directory to a `tokio::sync::Mutex`. The guard is moved into the `spawn_blocking` closure and dropped when the file work ends, so a start that gives up (fix round 1: at `TUNING_START_BOUND`, below) never lets a second writer in. No git call and no engine or manager lock is taken while it is held.
- **`tuning_io`.** `load` reads `<repo_dir>/tuning.toml`; a file that does not parse, or whose `v` is not 1 (`version <v> is not 1, the one this anthrex reads`), is renamed to `tuning.toml.bad-<now>`, with `-<n>` added while that name is taken. The parse error is put on one line, `line <n>: <toml's message>` (toml's own `Display` is a multi-line source snippet). An unreadable file (an I/O error other than not found) is neither moved nor overwritten: the start runs untuned. `save` is `toml::to_string` and `write_atomic`. `thresholds(repo_dir)` reads only, for triage. The restore's sweep names `TUNING_FILE` instead of the literal.
- **Run log.** `Tuned.log` from `tune_for_start` is, in order: the moved file's line, the refit-write lines (also to the daemon log, `tracing::info`), then `tuned`'s start lines; `build_run` opens the run's log with them (`at = now`). Every new run's log therefore starts with a `tuning:` line, `tuning: none (…)` when nothing is learned. When a start's refit wrote the weights, `tuning: path weights …` appears twice, as its refit-write line and its start line: decision 12 gives both the same text. A continued run's `based on …` line is still inserted first (`chain_goal.rs`).
- **`RunLimits`** gains `budget_hub`, `budget_configured`, `class_routes`, `path_weights` and `thresholds`, each `#[serde(default)]` and skipped while it holds what an older `run.json` reads it as, so `model_tuning_tests::an_old_run_json_still_loads` (9.3's `run.json` written back byte-equal) holds. `budget_configured` is a daemon-side copy, `model::BudgetsConfigured`, because `config::ConfiguredBudgets` has no serde; nothing reads it yet (the frozen `budget_s`/`budget_m` are already the effective ones). `RunLimits::freeze(tuned, config)` (in `model_limits.rs`) sets them and `budget_s`/`budget_m` to `Tuned::effective`. **Not added here:** `route_lists` (M9.5.10a's file list names `model_limits.rs` for it, and `RouteListsFrozen` does not exist yet) and decision 16's four settings (M9.5.13 owns their semantics, including what an older run reads).
- **The ceiling.** `ladder_budget::ceiling` calls `refit::ceiling(class, limits.budget_m, limits.budget_l)`. `refit::ceiling` (M9.5.8) has no token ceiling for M or hub when M has no token budget; `ladder_budget` then keeps L's token budget, so a config that sets tokens on L only behaves as before 9.5 (with `Tuned::default()` nothing changes). `refit.rs` is not touched.
- **Decider inputs.** `TriageInput.thresholds` and `SizeCheckInput.thresholds` are `#[serde(default)]` and skipped at the defaults. Triage reads the repository's thresholds in its existing blocking read (only when deciders are on; nothing is moved); the size check takes `run.limits.thresholds`.
- **Where the tests are.** `a_round_is_not_retuned` is in `engine/tests/tuning.rs`, not `driver/build_tests.rs`: the driver hands `RunRequest::Iterate` straight to the engine (`driver/requests.rs:165`), and nothing on that path builds a run, so the round-2 task's budget is the engine's to show (the fixture's next build says 80; the task gets the frozen 55). `a_planned_run_is_tuned_at_start` is in `driver/build_tests.rs` (it needs `build_delivered`'s I/O), not in the orch tests. `every_start_kind_is_tuned_once` starts a plan (`Shape::PlanFile`), a fast goal (`Shape::Fast`), a planned goal and a continued run (the untriaged `Shape::Planned` that `chain_goal.rs` passes to `build_delivered`) through `build_plan`. `rung_4_compares_within_the_budget_epoch` is pinning in substance (ruling T15-C1 already compares within the epoch); the extra `rung_4_ceiling_follows_the_frozen_m_budget` (an M task's ceiling is 400 calls over L's 300) failed before the change.
- **Extra tests:** `rung_4_ceiling_follows_the_frozen_m_budget`, `a_tuning_report_without_its_parse_error_still_decodes`. `two_concurrent_tunings_of_one_repo_serialise` refits the budgets in one start and the weights in the other (the history gives both starts the same classes, so two configs split the sections); with the lock replaced by a fresh mutex per call it fails on its first round.
- **Sizes.** `plan.rs` 548→565 (+17 of +25), `validate.rs` 535→536 (+8 −7), `driver.rs` 537→540 (+3), `driver/build.rs` 530→534 (+4), `engine/ladder_budget.rs` 122→135 (+13 of +15), `decider/mod.rs` 289→302 (+13 of +15), `decider/prompt.rs` 434→444 (+10 of +15), `orch/context.rs` 413→418, `orch/contract.rs` 511 (±0), `model_limits.rs` 114→153, `model_tuning.rs` 111→138, `driver/adapt_goal.rs` 471→475, `refit_render.rs` 101→108, `proto/src/tuning.rs` 258→261. New: `run/tuning_io.rs` 102, `run/driver/tuning.rs` 131, `daemon/tests/tuning_io.rs` 257, `run/plan_tests_tuning.rs` 205, `engine/tests/tuning.rs` 155, `decider/tests_thresholds.rs` 105; `driver/build_tests.rs` 192→345.
- **Fix round 1 (ruling T9-3, review minors).**
  - **One bound** (review I1): `TUNING_START_BOUND` (10 s, replacing `TUNE_TIMEOUT`'s 30 s) covers the wait for the repository's tuning lock and the file work together (`tune_within`, which `tune_for_start` calls with it; the guard still moves into the blocking closure). Past it the run starts untuned with the run log line `tuning: none (the tuning file was busy; started without what history taught)` (`TUNING_BUSY`). `RUN_START_TIMEOUT` and `CONTINUE_START_BOUND` add it by name (585 s; `GOAL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` 1215 s and `CONTINUE_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` 615 s follow), and the tests' `GOAL_WAIT` becomes 130 s (its worst case 112 + 10 s). `START_GOAL_TOOL_BOUND` is the tool's reply bound, not a sum, and is unchanged. `REQUEST_WAIT` (60 s) is kept: `run start`'s worst case in the harness is now exactly 60 s (50 s of git, 10 s of tuning); raising it would ripple through its derived rows and the smoke scripts' mirrors. `docs/timing-budgets.md` has the rows; the smoke scripts' comments carry the new sums (their constants still clear them).
  - **Every start gets its line:** a failed blocking task says `tuning: none (tuning did not finish: <error>; started without what history taught)`, an unreadable file `tuning: none (tuning.toml could not be read: <error>; started without what history taught)`. History off still writes none (no tuning is read).
  - **A goal tunes once:** `build_delivered` takes a `&TuneOnce` (`tokio::sync::OnceCell<Tuned>`); `start_goal` shares one between the fast build and the planned build it falls back to, so the planned run keeps the refit-write line its start wrote (`e2e_a_goal_that_falls_back_to_the_plan_path_is_tuned_once`). Every other caller passes a fresh one. `TuningLocks::tunings()` counts the starts' tunings; `every_start_kind_is_tuned_once` now asserts one per start.
  - **Deliberate readings, recorded:** a `tuning.toml` of another version (a newer anthrex's, on a downgrade) is moved aside as bad, like an unparseable one: this anthrex cannot tell what a later version's keys mean. The `TuningLocks` map keeps one entry per repository for the daemon's life (a few bytes each). The `tuning: path weights …` line appearing twice when a start's refit wrote the weights is decision 12's two texts being identical (review m7): left for a ruling.
  - New tests: `a_busy_tuning_file_starts_untuned_at_the_bound` (RED: with the lock's wait moved out of the bound, its 5 s guard fired), `an_unreadable_file_is_kept_and_the_log_says_so`, the e2e fallback test above (RED: the planned run's log lacked the refit-write line); `run_start_outwaits_both_preflights` and `the_continue_deadlines_are_run_starts_and_the_tools` pin the new sums (RED: 575 s against 585 s). `default_tuning_reproduces_today` drops its comparison of two default builds (review m5).
- **Fix round 2 (rulings T9-4, T9-5).** This supersedes fix round 1's `REQUEST_WAIT` sentence.
  - **`REQUEST_WAIT` is 75 s** (ruling T9-4): `run start`'s 50 s of git plus `TUNING_START_BOUND` (10 s) is 60 s, and one engine step and the reply make 75 s. Its derived bounds follow by their constants (`PR_START_WAIT` 440 s, `CONTINUE_WAIT` 195 s, `START_GOAL_WAIT` 165 s, `SUMMARY_WAIT` 375 s, `ITERATE_WAIT` 150 s, `GATE_SECS` 105 s), and `CONTINUE_WAIT`'s doc names the tuning term. `scripts/pty_smoke_keep_going.py`'s mirror is 75 s, with its comments' sums. `GOAL_WAIT` stays 130 s: in today's terms its worst case is 75 + 5 + 2 + 45 = 127 s.
  - **The start tests' `ask`** waits `ask_wait()`, derived from the daemon's constants as the CLI's `GOAL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` is: `CONTINUE_START_BOUND` (585 s), the default `deciders.timeout_secs` (90 s) and 30 s of the goal's own git, 705 s. The old 60 s sat below a start's legal worst case at the default `git_timeout_secs`.
  - **Each start line once** (ruling T9-5): `tune_blocking` appends a line of the start's `Tuned.log` only when the run log lacks that text, so a goal's planned fallback no longer writes `tuning: path weights …` twice. `a_planned_run_is_tuned_at_start` asserts the first three lines and one weights line; `run_e2e_adapt.rs`'s fallback pin expects exactly one of each.
  - **One settings read per goal:** `TuneOnce` also holds the orchestrator config. `start_goal` passes its own read (`TuneOnce::with_config`), so the fast build and the planned fallback both build from it, and neither reads the settings again. Test `a_shared_settings_read_is_the_builds`: with the live settings at the default and the shared config's S budget 33 calls 11m configured, the build's budget is 33/11 and its log line says `configured (refit would be 55 calls 18m)`.
  - `docs/timing-budgets.md`: every row derived from `REQUEST_WAIT`, the `ask` row, the `TUNING_START_BOUND` row, stage 11f's `run start` figure (593.25 s), and the `CONTINUE_WAIT` row, whose `GOAL_WAIT` sentence is corrected: a continue's own worst case is 140 s.
- **Fix round 3 (ruling T9-6; re-review N1).** This supersedes fix round 2's `ask` bullet. `delivery_tests_start.rs`'s services run `rig_config()`, the default with the harnesses' 5 s `git_timeout_secs`, and `ask_wait()` is a `pr` goal start's legal worst case on them. Its git calls are counted in the code: `GOAL_START_GIT_CALLS`, 39, which is `goal_ready`'s preflight 8, triage's `ls-files` 1, and two builds of 15 each (the fast one and its planned fallback). At 5 s each that is 195 s. To that it adds `PREFLIGHT_BOUND` (365 s), the default `deciders.timeout_secs` (90 s), the decider's `KILL_GRACE` (2 s), `TRIAGE_WRITE_TIMEOUT` (10 s, now `pub(in crate::run::driver)` and re-exported for tests), `TUNING_START_BOUND` (10 s), and 30 s for the engine step: 702 s. Fix round 2's 705 s summed the CLI's terms at the default 60 s timeout, which under-count the calls and leave out the kill grace. `docs/timing-budgets.md` rows 249 and 337 now say 593.25 s for `run start`, and two over-width comment lines are rewrapped. `RUN_REQUEST_TIMEOUT`'s own under-count (the CLI) is left as it was, for the controller.

### Task M9.5.10a (2026-10-03): model lists for tasks

Decision 9a for the task classes and the `review` list, ruling RL-2 for task picks, and the controller addendum T9-2 (`build_run` freezes the lists into `RunLimits.route_lists`).

- **The frozen lists** (`model_tuning.rs`): `RouteListsFrozen` (one `FrozenList` per table, `ListCandidate { runtime, model, strength, effort }` with the roster's strength, `ListPolicy::{First, Spread}`), built by `RouteListsFrozen::freeze(&tuned.lists, &config.models)` in `RunLimits::freeze`. Empty lists and the field itself are skipped when empty, so a run without lists writes 9.3's `run.json`: `model_tuning_tests::NEW_KEYS` now counts `route_lists`, `list_pick` and `list_escalation` (zero in `an_old_run_json_still_loads`, present in the round trip), and `an_old_run_json_has_no_lists` reads the protocol-15 fixture.
- **Two `Task` fields beyond Interfaces**, both `#[serde(default)]` and skipped while `None`: `list_pick: Option<ListPick>`, the plan's choice (the list snapshot with each candidate's skip reason, the chosen index or `None`, the pick and a `spread` slot); and `list_escalation`, rung 2's list step, which the next worker launch records and clears like `escalated_from`. The plan's choice and rung 2's are kept apart so rule 9 keeps reading the plan's runtime (below), never an escalated one (final review A-I1).
- **Signatures differ from Interfaces**, for what the code needs: `pick(limits, roster, tasks, targets, installed)` (the class's default effort is in `limits.class_routes`, and a re-routed task's reviewer is picked again, which needs the roster), with `pick_all` (the build) and `pick_named(run, ids)` (an edit batch); `next_candidate(limits, tasks, i, installed)`; `reviewer(lists, author, level, installed) -> Option<(Option<Route>, Vec<RoutingCandidate>)>` (`None`: no list; an inner `None`: none qualifies, the caller takes `pick_reviewer`), with `review_route(..)` wrapping both. `installed` is `run.orch.installed` as it is stored, by runtime label (`route_pick::Installed`), and a runtime it does not name counts as installed.
- **Readings.** A plan route is explicit when it names a model or a strength; a route naming a runtime (and perhaps an effort) takes the list narrowed to that runtime (`the task names the runtime <rt>` for the others), and a plan's effort replaces the candidate's. Picks are made per **overlap group** (connected `owns` within a class) for `first` as well as `spread`, and a candidate is skipped as `overlapping owns` when an unfinished task already routed on another runtime (an explicit route, another class, an earlier group) overlaps a member: that is what makes contract rule 22 hold by construction against explicit routes too. A `spread` group joins the slot and candidate of an unfinished, list-routed task of its class it overlaps, else takes the class's next slot (one past the highest recorded). The reviewer's floor is the higher of the author's strength and the level's ("at or above the author's and the review level's"); a same-runtime candidate is skipped as `the author's runtime`. Rung 2 follows the class list for every task of a listed class (explicit routes too), finds the current candidate by its exact route, uses the candidate's effort else the class's, and skips `the current route`. Research and review tasks are left to M9.5.10b's role lists. With every candidate skipped, the route is today's resolution and the snapshot is still recorded (a fallback is appended by `routing::select`).
- **Installed at a build** is not known: a plan-file run never records it and a planned run records it after its build (`make_planned`); a planned run's tasks arrive by edits, which read `run.orch.installed`.
- **Beyond the file list.** `validate_graph::planned_runtime` (+4) reads a list's pick as the plan's runtime, so rule 9 refuses a task added on another runtime over a listed task's `owns` (`the_overlap_rule_sees_a_lists_runtime`). `reach.rs` (+5) counts every class-list candidate as a starting route, because rung 2 can take any of them (`a_class_lists_runtimes_are_reachable`). Both tests failed with those two files reverted. The reviewer forecasts (`validate::resolve_task_lenient`, `edits_amend::reresolve`, `ladder::reresolve`, `deciders_size`) go through `route_pick::review_route`; the reviewer launched is `review_ready`'s.
- **History.** `routing.rs` gains only the call (`record_worker` asks `routing_lists::worker` first); the list decisions are in `routing_lists.rs` (a child module, for `routing.rs`'s +30): source `configured_list` with policy `m9.5-list-v1` when the list chose the route; an explicit route `explicit_task` (policy `m9.5-list-v1`); a fallback `class_default`/`m8a-worker-v1` or `escalation_policy`/`m8a-escalate-v1`; `pick_policy` the list's, or `first_qualifying` for a reviewer (`record_listed_reviewer`; with none qualifying, `review_policy`/`m8a-review-v1`). "Unchanged after a config reload": decisions are recorded at choice time from the run's frozen copy and never recomputed; the tests show an edit after a `run.json` round trip uses the run's copy and a later edit leaves an earlier decision as it was (the engine has no config-reload event to drive).
- **Planners.** `get_context` gains `limits.routes` only when a list exists (every non-empty list, classes and roles, `effort` only where set; `route_pick::context_routes`). The contract sentence is appended to orchestrator rule 21 and planner rule 7, unconditionally (they are constants); the pins in `orch/contract_tests.rs` are updated.
- **Not here.** `run retry`'s re-pick (`engine/requests.rs`) and the failed-in-this-task skip are M9.5.10b's; the action menu's retry preview (`actions/effects.rs:166`) and the bisect and delivery fix escalations still use `roster::escalate`.
- **Tests.** `route_pick_tests.rs` (547) and `route_pick_tests_frozen.rs` (178, split for the 600-line rule) hold the brief's list plus `targets_are_the_named_unfinished_tasks`, `an_old_run_json_has_no_lists` and the two above; the reducer-driven ones are `engine/tests/route_lists.rs` (`a_listed_worker_records_its_list_and_rung_2_takes_the_next_candidate`, `the_reviewer_comes_from_the_review_list_and_records_it`). `no_lists_reproduce_today` is pinning, with the existing route tests unchanged.
- **Sizes.** `route_pick.rs` 421 (new), `routing_lists.rs` 86 (new), `model_tuning.rs` 138→296, `model_limits.rs` 153→158, `model_task.rs` 220→228, `plan.rs` 565→569 (+21 of +25 in all), `edits.rs` 432→440 (+8 of +10), `edits_amend.rs` 208→214, `routing.rs` 359→366, `engine/ladder.rs` 443→450 (+7 of 10a's +12), `engine/review.rs` 378→387, `orch/context.rs` 418→422, `orch/contract.rs` 511, `validate.rs` 536→541 (the milestone's +8 for `validate.rs` is now +15 over `c8d77428`'s 526, of which M9.5.3b's literal fields are 9), `validate_graph.rs` 436→440, `reach.rs` 104→109, `engine/deciders_size.rs` 230.

- **Fix round 1 (rulings T10a-1, T10a-2; review minors m1–m7), after task M9.5.10b.** Supersedes the rung-2 and `spread` readings above.
  - **T10a-1.** `next_candidate` builds the candidates' routes with `route_for` (the plan's effort, as `pick` gave them), finds the current candidate by its exact route, else by runtime and model, and returns `None` when the list does not hold the current route (an explicit route outside it), so rung 2 and `run retry` take `roster::escalate`; it never restarts at the first candidate. It also skips, last, a candidate `below the current route` (`BELOW_CURRENT`): a weaker strength, or a lower effort at the same strength; a stronger model at a lower effort is a step up. A list's own order may therefore not be followed to a weaker entry: from its last candidate rung 2 cycles past weaker ones, else falls back to `escalate`. Test `rung_2_never_steps_down` (a listed route with a plan effort, a listed route at the end of its list, an explicit route the list lacks, a one-candidate list); `rung_2_takes_the_next_candidate_and_never_breaks_the_overlap_rule`'s cycling case and `engine/tests/route_lists.rs`'s list (its second candidate is now opus, not sonnet) follow the guard. `a_class_lists_runtimes_are_reachable` keeps its reach assertion; `reach.rs` counting every list candidate is now a superset.
  - **T10a-2.** Under `spread`, a group whose member names a runtime starts at the first candidate (narrowed to that runtime by `skip`) and takes no slot, so later groups keep theirs. Test `spread_gives_a_runtime_only_route_its_runtimes_first_candidate_and_no_slot` (t3 claude-only between groups: opus with no slot, t4 slot 2).
  - **m1.** A decider's raise (`deciders_size::apply_raise`) keeps the effort of a route its list chose, as it keeps a plan's (`a_decider_raise_keeps_a_listed_effort`). **m2.** An amend that changes a listed task's class without naming a route re-picks from the new class's list (`route_pick::same_list`, `edits_amend.rs`; `an_amend_that_changes_the_class_picks_from_the_new_classs_list`). **m3.** Risks 16. **m4.** `a_workers_decision_records_a_candidate_not_installed` puts `not installed` in a worker's `RoutingDecision` (coverage, passes as written; task M9.5.10b's `escalation_and_review_history_keep_skips` has the reviewer's `below the author's strength` and `not installed`). **m5.** `reach.rs` forecasts reviewers with `route_pick::review_route`; no test can fail without it, because a qualifying `review` candidate is always on the author's peer runtime, where `pick_reviewer` also lands. **m6.** `validate.rs` takes the forecast through `route_pick::forecast` in the line it replaced: 538, +2 over `03dc0e91` (the two `Task` fields). **m7.** `limits.routes` also gives each candidate's `strength` (the roster's), beyond decision 9a's runtime, model and effort, so a planner can tell the candidates apart without the roster.
  - Sizes: `route_pick.rs` 542→580, `edits_amend.rs` 214→218, `engine/deciders_size.rs` 230→232, `reach.rs` 113→114, `validate.rs` 541→538; tests `route_pick_tests.rs` 551→564, `route_pick_tests_fixes.rs` 201 (new), `engine/tests/route_lists.rs` 188→222.
- **Fix round 2 (rulings T10a-3, T10a-4; re-review N1, N2), after 10b's fix round.** This supersedes fix round 1's reading that "never weaker" holds for every rung-2 step, and m1's raise rule.
  - **T10a-3.** When the current route failed in this task (`environment_failed`), the next pick is a substitution. `next_candidate` drops the `below the current route` skip, so it takes the next list candidate that has not failed, whatever its strength; the not-installed, failed and overlap skips stay. Without one, `rung2_route` takes `roster_skipping::escalate_skipping`. When that returns a failed route (its silent fallback), rung 2 and `run retry` retry the task's own route instead. `route_pick::every_route_failed` gives the line `every route for task <id> failed in this task; retrying <runtime>/<model>`, which `ladder::rung2` and `requests::rung2` write to the run log (the retry preview in `actions/effects.rs` does not log). A non-environment rung 2 keeps "never weaker" (`rung_2_never_steps_down`). A failed explicit route that the list does not hold still goes to the roster (T10a-1), skipping failed routes. Tests: `a_failed_route_is_substituted_by_the_next_unfailed_candidate` and `with_every_route_failed_the_original_is_retried_and_said` (pure, `route_pick_tests_fixes.rs`). The new `engine/tests/route_lists_substitute.rs` covers [opus, codex Standard] with opus failed by a `ClientError`: `run_retry_substitutes_…`, `rung_2_substitutes_…` (through `ladder::rung2`) and `with_every_route_failed_a_retry_takes_the_original_and_logs_it`.
  - **T10a-4.** One rule, `route_pick::repicks(old, new)`, replaces `same_list`: the old task took a list's candidate and the new one takes another list. `edits_amend.rs` and `deciders_size::apply_raise` both call it. On a decider's S→M raise that re-picks, the route becomes the M resolution, `pick_named` picks again from the M list (an empty list leaves that resolution and no `list_pick`), and the reviewer is re-forecast. `a_decider_raise_repicks_from_the_new_classes_list` replaces `a_decider_raise_keeps_a_listed_effort`. A listed effort is still kept when the raise stays on the same list (S and M share no list, so this covers a hub task).
  - Sizes: `route_pick.rs` 580→602 (`repicks` replaced `same_list`; at the bound), `edits_amend.rs` 218→217, `engine/deciders_size.rs` 232→246, `ladder.rs` 446→449, `requests.rs` 422→425; tests `route_pick_tests_fixes.rs` 201→275, `engine/tests/route_lists.rs` 222→242, `engine/tests/route_lists_substitute.rs` 130 (new).
- **Fix round 3 (ruling T10a-5; re-review N3), after task 11.**
  - **The split.** A move-only commit splits `next_candidate`, `below`, `rung2_route` and `every_route_failed` out of `route_pick.rs` (602→499 lines) into the child `route_pick_step.rs`, which `route_pick` re-exports. The lib suite counts 2888 tests before and after. One run had a timing failure in `driver::tier::steps_run_in_plan_order_each_holding_its_slots`, which passed on 3 reruns.
  - **T10a-5.** When the current route failed in this task and the list gives no step (no list, an explicit route the list does not hold, or no list candidate left), `rung2_route` takes `route_pick_step::roster_substitute`, not `escalate_skipping`. It picks from the roster entries that have not failed in this task and whose runtime is installed: the peer runtime's first entry at the same strength, else the strongest left. The original is retried, and the `every route … failed` line written, only when that set is empty. A route that did not fail keeps the roster escalation, which is never weaker.
  - **Readings, recorded.**
    - On a tie among the strongest, the peer runtime's entry comes first, then roster order. In the default roster, a failed opus therefore goes to Codex's Standard default, not to sonnet: an environment failure such as billing or login is usually runtime-wide.
    - The substitute runs at `high` effort, as `escalate_skipping` steps on from a failed route. 10b's preview test, `codex default (high effort)`, is unchanged.
    - A runtime that is not installed is skipped, as the lists skip it. The line's "every route" therefore means every installed route.
  - **Tests.**
    - Pure, in `route_pick_tests_fixes.rs`: `with_no_list_a_failed_route_is_substituted_from_the_roster` (not failed escalates to opus high; failed takes the Frontier peer gpt-6.1-sol; that failed too takes Codex's default; all failed takes the original and the line) and `a_failed_unlisted_explicit_route_is_substituted_from_the_roster`.
    - Engine, in `engine/tests/route_lists_substitute.rs` (default roster, opus): `with_no_list_run_retry_…`, `with_no_list_rung_2_…` and `with_every_roster_route_failed_a_retry_takes_the_original_and_logs_it`.
    - RED: the pure staged test and the two engine substitution tests. The explicit-route and every-roster-route tests passed before the change. Both keep behaviour the change could break: escalation already takes a same-strength peer, and the all-failed line already fired.
  - **Sizes.** `route_pick.rs` 499, `route_pick_step.rs` 148 (new); tests `route_pick_tests_fixes.rs` 275→329, `engine/tests/route_lists_substitute.rs` 130→212.
- **Fix round 4 (ruling T10a-6; re-review 3's two pre-existing observations), after task 11's fix round.**
  - **The rule.** Both of rung 2's roster paths now take only `route_pick_step::open_roster`'s entries. This covers `escalate_skipping` for a route that did not fail and `roster_substitute` for one that did. `open_roster` keeps every entry on the task's own runtime. An entry on another runtime it keeps only when that runtime is installed for the run and no unfinished task on a different runtime overlaps this task's `owns`: the lists' `overlapping owns` skip, generalised to any runtime (M8a decision 11, contract rule 22). With nothing open on another runtime, rung 2 escalates within its own runtime as before. `roster_substitute` no longer filters by installed runtime itself; `open_roster` does.
  - **One reading, recorded.** When the substitute finds nothing because the overlap rule holds the task off a runtime that still has a route that has not failed, the original is retried. The log line is then `no route for task <id> keeps the overlap rule and has not failed in this task; retrying <runtime>/<model>`, not the `every route …` line, which would be false. `every_route_failed` tells the two apart by running the substitute again without the overlap rule.
  - **Out of scope.** `roster::escalate`'s other callers apply neither skip: the bisect fix task (`engine/bisect.rs:552`), the CI fix task (`delivery/fix.rs:312`) and the reach forecast (`reach.rs:78`).
  - **Tests** (`route_pick_tests_fixes.rs`; both RED, then GREEN):
    - `a_roster_substitute_keeps_the_overlap_rule`. `t1`'s failed opus, overlapping an unfinished `t2` on Claude, takes sonnet (high), not the Codex Frontier peer. With every Claude route failed, the original and the overlap line. With `t2` merged, the Codex peer.
    - `a_gate_path_rung_2_keeps_to_installed_and_overlap_free_runtimes`. Sonnet at high goes to Codex's default. With Codex not installed it goes to opus (high), and likewise with an overlapping unfinished Claude task.
  - **Sizes.** `route_pick_step.rs` 148→173; `route_pick_tests_fixes.rs` 329→382.

### Task M9.5.10b (2026-10-03): model lists for roles; what is installed or just failed

Decision 9a for the run's roles, the orchestrator, onboarding scouts and deciders; rulings RL-1 to RL-4, RH-5's precedence, review ruling I6. The Settings note (`overridden by [orchestrator.routes.orchestrator]`) is M9.5.11's.

- **Failed in this task (RL-1).** A route failed in a task when one of that task's rounds carries `AgentRound.environment_failed` (new, `#[serde(default)]`, skipped while `false`; `model_tuning_tests::NEW_KEYS` counts it). It is set where a session's own failed turn blocks the task `blocked(environment)`: `signals.rs::failed_turn` (sandbox unavailable; auth, billing, client error; `Other` after the continue), the reviewer's block arm in `review_session.rs`, and a research session's give-up in `research.rs`. A route is identified by runtime and model, so a failed route is skipped at every effort. `route_pick::failed_routes(task)` reads it; `next_candidate` and `reviewer` skip it as `failed in this task` (after `not installed`); with no list, `roster::pick_reviewer_skipping` and `roster::escalate_skipping` pick over the roster without it (a route that itself failed escalates as from `high`, so never to a higher effort on the same model). A route that failed in another task is not skipped. `routing.rs::mark_failed` names the skip in the fallback pools `record_worker` and `record_reviewer` record.
- **One rung-2 route.** `route_pick::rung2_route(run, i)` (the list's next candidate, else `escalate_skipping`) is what rung 2 (`ladder.rs`), `run retry` (`requests.rs`) and the action menu's retry preview (`actions/effects.rs`) all take, so the preview names the route a retry takes (`the_retry_preview_names_the_route_a_retry_takes`). It lives in `route_pick.rs`, not `ladder.rs`, for `ladder.rs`'s budget. A retried research or review task stores the step as its `list_pick` (its review records it, `a_retried_review_task_takes_and_records_its_lists_next_candidate`); a worker's goes to `list_escalation` as in 10a.
- **Research and review tasks (RL-4)** route by `route_pick::task_list`: research by `scout`, review tasks by `review`, at that list's pick, except that a review task always takes `first` (its target has no owns to spread over). This is in `route_pick.rs::pick`, not `orch/launch.rs` as the file list says: the task picks are made there. `review_ready` records a review task's list when its route is still the list's choice. The integration review also goes through `review_route` (`engine/integration.rs`). A research session still records no routing decision (as before 10b).
- **Roles (`orch/role_lists.rs`, new, a child of `roles.rs`).** `scout_pick` (rotation: the scout's place in `run.orch.run_scouts`, start order) and `planner_pick` (rotation: the epic index) over the run's frozen lists and `run.orch.installed`; `launch.rs::scout_route_of(run, scout_id)` and `planner_route` take them first, else today's rule (`frozen_scout_route`). The role records keep the list snapshot, `pick_policy`, and for `spread` the rotation: **`RoleRoutingDecision.rotation: Option<u32>`** is a new proto field (`#[serde(default)]`, skipped while `None`; additive, so `PROTO_VERSION` stays 16; `a_role_decisions_rotation_round_trips`). `reach.rs` counts the `scout` and `planner` lists' runtimes as starting routes when the run has an orchestrator.
- **The orchestrator (RH-5, RL-3).** `role_lists::orchestrator(choice, list, effort, installed, today)`: an explicit `OrchestratorChoice` wins; else the `orchestrator` list's first candidate not skipped; else `today` (decision 6's resolution from `[orchestrator.agent]`), with the skipped list candidates leading its snapshot. A continued chain keeps its route because its continuation passes that route as the choice (`chain_goal.rs`), so the chain's precedence needs no code of its own; `a_continued_chain_keeps_its_route_over_the_orchestrator_list` checks the run `continued_run` builds, since adoption then copies the previous orchestrator's record wholesale (source included). `orch/installed.rs` gains `resolve_planned` (the start, over the window map) and `resolve_promoted` (`run promote`, over the headless map), which `driver/build.rs` and `engine/promote.rs` call.
- **The installed probe (RL-2, I6).** `found_within`, `INSTALLED_PROBE_TIMEOUT` (5 s), `installed_of` and `installed_now` are in `driver/build_installed.rs`, a new child module of `build.rs` (`build.rs` was already over its budget at 562; it is 555 now); `found` and `Found` are `pub(crate)`. `found_within` runs the probe on `spawn_blocking` under `tokio::time::timeout`; on a timeout or join error it returns `None` and the caller resolves as today (nothing installed known: every runtime counts as installed). Nothing caches the result.
- **Onboarding scouts.** `profile/service_start.rs::scout_checks` probes at the onboarding start and routes the scout with `scout/spec.rs::onboarding_route(ctx, list, rotation, installed)` over `[orchestrator.routes.scout]`; the rotation is a per-service `onboarding_rotation` counter (`ProfileService`), since onboarding has no run to count scouts in. The route rides the `Job` (`route: Option<Route>`) to `service_run.rs`, which starts the scout on it.
- **Deciders.** `DeciderContext` gains `routing: call::Routing` (the frozen-at-construction list, the roster, the bins and a per-daemon rotation counter). The probe and the pick are `call::routed(ctx)` / `call::route_over(ctx, installed)`, called by `adapt.rs::decide_as` and `adapt_goal.rs`'s triage before the dispatch record, so the record names the route the call takes; `decide` itself is unchanged. `Off` is untouched. With no list, a decider whose runtime is not installed moves to the peer runtime's ladder route when the peer is installed, else stays as today. The record is `roles::decider_listed`.
- **Timing bounds.** The probe adds at most `INSTALLED_PROBE_TIMEOUT` before a goal's triage: `cli/src/run_cmd/adapt.rs::GOAL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` is 1220 s, `cli/tests/support/run_adapt.rs::GOAL_WAIT` 140 s, `delivery_tests_start.rs`'s `ask_wait` 707 s; `scripts/pty_smoke_adapt.py`'s comment; `docs/timing-budgets.md`'s rows for each, and a new "Recorded, from M9.5.10b" row for `the_installed_probe_gives_up_after_its_timeout` (bound 50 ms, the probe blocks 500 ms).
- **Beyond the file list.** `engine/{integration, promote, review, review_session, research, signals, rounds, actions/effects}.rs`, `run/{routing, reach, model_rounds, model_tuning}.rs`, `orch/installed.rs`, `driver/{adapt, adapt_goal, chain_goal, orch_ops}.rs`, `profile/{service, service_requests}.rs`, the proto history type, the literal fixtures that gained a field (`orch/test_support.rs`, `edits_tests.rs`, `report_tests.rs`, `tests/run_journal/fixture.rs`, `scout/tests.rs`, `decider/tests_argv.rs`, `fake-agent/tests/adapt_modes.rs`) and the timing bounds above. `validate.rs` is untouched.
- **Tests.** `route_pick_tests_roles.rs` (the brief's `retry_skips_…`, `escalation_and_review_history_keep_skips`, `research_routes_…`, `scouts_spread_over_their_list`, plus `a_role_lists_runtimes_are_reachable`); `orch/role_lists_tests.rs` (`the_orchestrator_precedence`, `role_routing_history_keeps_the_list_and_rotation`, `run_scouts_spread_over_their_list_in_start_order`); `engine/tests/route_lists_failed.rs` (reducer-driven: a reviewer's failure re-routes the next reviewer, the retry preview, a retried review task); `driver/adapt_goal_list_tests.rs` and `driver/chain_goal_list_tests.rs` (the precedence through `build_plan` and a chain); `driver/build_installed_tests.rs`; `decider/tests_route.rs` (`deciders_use_their_list_unless_off`, `deciders_route_over_what_is_installed_at_each_call`); `profile/tests_installed.rs` (`onboarding_scouts_and_deciders_use_what_is_installed`, with `ANTHREX_CLAUDE_BIN` nonexistent and `ANTHREX_CODEX_BIN` the fake agent). `no_lists_reproduce_today` passes unchanged.
- **Sizes.** `route_pick.rs` 421→542, `roster.rs` 155→182 (**+27 of +20**: the two `_skipping` variants and `without`), `scout/spec.rs` 289→306 (**4 over** the brief's 287+15), `orch/launch.rs` 409→415 (of 418), `orch/roles.rs` 370→417, `orch/installed.rs` 96→128, `driver/build.rs` 562→555, `decider/call.rs` 275→399, `decider/mod.rs` 302→295, `engine/ladder.rs` 450→446, `engine/requests.rs` 417→422, `routing.rs` 366→377, `reach.rs` 109→113; new `orch/role_lists.rs` 93, `driver/build_installed.rs` 53.
- **Fix round 1 (ruling T10b-1; review minors 1–6).** Built on 10a's fix round (4616d033).
  - **T10b-1 (minor 1).** A decider still moves to the peer runtime when the probe finds its mode's runtime not installed and the peer installed, an explicit `[orchestrator.deciders] mode` included. `call::Routed.moved` keeps the mode's route the call moved from (only when no list candidate was chosen). `roles::decider_listed` records it first, skipped as `not installed`, ahead of the peer's candidates. `Routed::moved_line` is the run log's `decider: <runtime> is not installed; using <peer>`. It rides the record to the engine (`OrchEvent::RoleRoute.log`, an in-memory event field, no protocol change) and is logged when the record is kept (`role_routes::keep`). A triage has no run yet, so its line goes to the daemon log. Tests: `a_decider_whose_runtime_is_not_installed_moves_and_says_so` (the driver, a real engine) and `deciders_route_over_what_is_installed_at_each_call` (the pure half).
  - **Minor 2.** `routing.rs::mark_failed` leaves the chosen model unmarked: when every entry failed and the selector fell back to a failed model, the record no longer calls that model `failed in this task` (`escalation_and_review_history_keep_skips`, extended).
  - **Minor 3.** A continued chain's orchestrator record has source `continued_chain` (`role_lists::CHAIN_SOURCE`), set by `chain_goal.rs` over the `explicit_choice` its passed choice resolved to. RH-5 ranks a chain as its own level, and history now tells it from a user's choice. The proto and `RoleSnapshot` docs list `continued_chain` and `configured_list`.
  - **Minor 4.** "Failed in this task" is every environment-failed round of the task, not only the latest. A route stays excluded for the rest of the task, even after the environment is fixed and the task retried. Fixing the environment and starting a new task is the way back to that route.
  - **Minor 5.** `roster.rs`'s skipping picks moved to `run/roster_skipping.rs` (a child module, 34 lines), so `roster.rs` is 160 (+5 of +20). `onboarding_route` moved to `profile/service_start.rs` (its one caller), so `scout/spec.rs` is back at 289. `decider/call.rs` is 415 (no stated bound).
  - **Minor 6.** `[orchestrator.routes]` is not a settings-owned key (`config::settings::apply_owned`), so no save changes a list while the daemon runs. The onboarding scout freezes the daemon's `scout` list against the live roster at each start, the same roster its fallback reads. The deciders fix both list and roster at daemon start, as their route always was. Both are documented at the code.
  - Sizes: `routing.rs` 377→382, `orch/roles.rs` 417→424, `role_lists.rs` 93→97, `driver/adapt.rs` 538→540, `adapt_goal.rs` 484→489, `chain_goal.rs` 341→347, `engine/role_routes.rs` 294→299, `engine/orch.rs` 593→594, `orch_event.rs` 164→167, `profile/service_start.rs` 206→223.

### Task M9.5.11 (2026-10-03): `run stats` apply and dismiss; the TUI's tuning block and Settings notes

Decisions 11 (I/O half) and 48 and rulings RH-5, RH-6, RH-7 and T8-2 as written, with these readings:

- **`stats_with_tuning(config, locks, repo_dir, apply, dismiss, read_only, now)`** (in `driver/tuning.rs`, as Interfaces places it) takes the request's `&config::Orchestrator`, not `&RunContext`, as `tune_for_start` does (task 9). It holds the repository's tuning lock, and one bound covers the lock's wait and the file work: `TUNING_START_BOUND`, reused. Past it, a request with `--apply` or `--dismiss` ids is refused with `TUNING_STATS_BUSY` (`the tuning file stayed busy (a run is starting); try again`), and a plain one answers without its tuning block. Any other tuning failure is handled the same way: an unreadable file, an unwritable one, or a blocking task that did not finish. `driver/adapt_history.rs::stats` calls it after the history summary, from the repository data directory `stats_of` now returns. `docs/timing-budgets.md` has the note ("Recorded, from M9.5.11").
- **Order and refusal.** The file is loaded, refitted from history, and the ids are checked against the proposals still current (`refit::apply`, then `refit::dismiss`). Only then is anything saved, once. So an unknown id, in either list, refuses the whole request. In that case nothing is written, not even the refit, though a bad file has already been moved aside at load.
- **Read-only** (decision 48). `history_io::summarise` is split: `summarise_read` reads and aggregates with no git and no write, and `summarise` records the reverts and then calls it. `tuning_io::peek` reads the file without moving it. A read-only `Stats` therefore runs only the checkout's git. The report is over the file as it is, so `RefitState` is what the file has. Each class's `refit_budget` is taken from a report over the in-memory refit, which is what a plain `run stats` would write. A file that does not parse is left in place: the report carries `parse_error` and no `moved_bad_file`. A read-only `Stats` that names ids is refused before anything is read: `a read-only stats request cannot apply or dismiss a proposal` (`adapt_history::READ_ONLY_CHANGES`). `daemon/tests/server_runs.rs`'s task-2 pin, which said the three fields were ignored until this task, now pins that refusal. It is renamed `mcp_ready_is_answered_and_a_read_only_stats_takes_no_ids`.
- **T8-2.** A plain `run stats` that moves a bad file prints decision 10's line with `(<error>)`, through `refit_render::moved_bad_line`. Extra test: `a_moved_bad_file_says_why_in_stats`, which also checks that a read-only request leaves the file where it is.
- **CLI.**
  - The flags are a flattened `run_cmd/adapt.rs::TuningFlags`. `--apply` and `--dismiss` each take one or more ids and can repeat. This keeps `run_cmd.rs` at +2.
  - With ids, the CLI first fetches the current proposals with a plain `Stats`. It asks `apply <id>: <text>? [y/N] ` per applied id that is current, unless `--yes`. It asks through `finish.rs::ask_yes`, made `pub(super)`, with the hint `stdin is not a terminal; pass --yes`. Then it sends the ids answered yes, with every `--dismiss` id. An id that is not current is sent without asking, so the daemon refuses with its own text and the CLI exits 1.
  - If every asked id is answered no and nothing is dismissed, the CLI exits 1 with `nothing applied`, as `run accept`'s no does (`not merged`).
  - `applied <id>: new runs in <project> use it` names the `--dir` resolved and canonical, or the current directory; there is no project in `TuningReport`. From a subdirectory, or from a linked worktree, it names that directory, not the main checkout.
  - `dismissed <id>: … would propose <value>` takes `<value>` from the fetched proposal.
  - With `--json`, the `applied` and `dismissed` lines go to stderr, so stdout stays JSON.
  - Text mode prints `stats::render`, then `refit_render::render(tuning)`. `run_e2e_tiers.rs::e2e_run_stats_proposes_a_thrice_flaky_test` asserted that the output ends with the flaky block. It now asserts that the flaky block is followed by `tuning: `.
- **The stats screen** (`ui/stats.rs::tuning_lines`) draws `refit_render::render`'s lines after the flaky block, each one sanitised (`one_line`) and folded to ASCII in an ASCII palette (`→` becomes `->`). The `*` note and the `budget <C>: configured …` lines are muted. The `CLASS` header and `tuning proposals:` are bold, like the screen's other headings. `apply with …` is replaced by `apply: anthrex run stats --apply <id>` (`APPLY_HINT`). The hints and keys are unchanged.
- **Settings.**
  - The read-only `Stats` is sent from `open_settings` (`app/settings_flow.rs`, where opening lives; +45), not from `settings_screen.rs`, which gains the two fields and their helpers (+23). It is sent for `goal_project()`, both when the screen opens from the cache and when it is still loading.
  - Its reply is routed first in `route_stats_reply`, by the screen's `tuning_request`. It is registered as `PendingWhat::Stats`, so an expiry is quiet. A refusal draws nothing. A failed send is quiet too: `tagged_not_sent` counts it as the screen's own, in `replies.rs` (+1).
  - Two app tests that asserted "opening from the cache sends nothing" now assert that no `Settings` request is sent.
  - **The refit note is a line of its own**, not on the budget's row. It sits under the class's `minutes` row, in the value column: `                    refit: 55 calls 18m`, muted. At 80 columns the `s tool calls` row (`at least 1   hard stop at 60 calls`) leaves 2 cells for it, so the note was cut to `refit: 5`.
  - The note is not a row the keys select. `limit_lines` returns the selected limit's line index, counting the notes above it, so the section's scroll keeps the selection in view (`the_refit_note_leaves_the_selection_on_its_limit`, which fails with the old index).
  - The note is drawn for S and M only, the classes Settings has budgets for: `ClassTuning.refit_budget` set and `configured` false. The text is `daemon::run::refit::budget_text`, so tokens appear as the CLI prints them.
  - `overridden by [orchestrator.routes.orchestrator]` gets the same treatment: a muted line under the orchestrator default's two rows (`runtime`, `model`), in their value column. Beside `runtime ‹ configured ›  (default)` it would be cut at 80 columns. It comes after the two selectable rows, so the selection index is unchanged.
- **Audit.** The `stats` fixture now carries the tuning block (`history_with_tuning`). A new fixture, `settings limits with a refit`, has the S note on the limits section and the orchestrator list set. Its row is in `audit::shows`. Every fixture check passes, the ASCII one included.
- **Test placement.** The brief lists `settings_shows_the_orchestrator_list_override` with "muted", so it is a TUI test in `settings_tuning_tests.rs`. Its daemon half is `run_stats_tuning.rs::a_read_only_stats_reports_the_orchestrator_list`.
- **Extra tests:**
  - `a_moved_bad_file_says_why_in_stats`;
  - `a_read_only_stats_reports_the_orchestrator_list`;
  - `the_refit_note_leaves_the_selection_on_its_limit`;
  - `the_notes_render_in_ascii_and_leave_the_stats_screen_alone`.
- **Sizes.**
  - Within their bounds: `run_cmd.rs` 549→551 (+2 of +8), `ui/stats.rs` 248→285 (+37 of +40), `app/settings_screen.rs` 492→515 (+23 of +25), `ui/settings.rs` 504→533 (+29 of +30, of which 4 are the test module's declaration), `app/stats.rs` 208→212.
  - Over its bound: `run_cmd/adapt.rs` 234→354 (+120 against +70: the flags struct 14, the asking and printing about 75, and the help test 31).
  - Not bounded: `driver/tuning.rs` 182→277, `driver/adapt_history.rs` 150→192, `history_io.rs` 471→482, `tuning_io.rs` 102→111, `app/settings_flow.rs` 217→262.
  - New: `cli/tests/run_stats_tuning.rs` 460, `tui/src/ui/settings_tuning_tests.rs` 225; `ui/stats_tests.rs` 397→535.
- **Fix round 1 (review I1, ruling T11-1, review minors).** This supersedes the CLI bullet's `<project>` reading and the busy bullet above.
  - **I1.** `run stats --apply` or `--dismiss` fetches the proposals first. When that reply has no tuning block, the CLI asks nothing, sends no id, and exits 1 with `tuning is not available right now (see the daemon's log); nothing applied or dismissed` (`adapt.rs::TUNING_UNAVAILABLE`). This holds with `--yes` too. Before the fix, a missing block meant every id was sent without its question. The daemon leaves the block out of a plain stats on any tuning failure, `TUNING_STATS_BUSY` among them. Test: `apply_without_a_tuning_block_refuses_and_sends_nothing`, where `tuning.toml` is a directory. It was RED: the id went out unasked, and the daemon refused it with `tuning.toml could not be read`.
  - **Ruling T11-1.** The Settings notes stay on their own muted line under their rows, in the value column, not beside them as RH-5 and decision 48 word it. The reason is legibility at 80 columns: on its own row the refit note was cut to `refit: 5`, and the override note is cut beside `runtime ‹ configured ›  (default)`.
  - **The repository named.** `TuningReport` gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] project: Option<PathBuf>`: the main checkout the daemon keys the repository by (`checkout_of`'s project, which `stats_of` now returns). Protocol stays 16, unmerged, as ruling T8-2 set for `parse_error`. Test: `a_tuning_report_without_its_project_still_decodes`. `applied <id>: new runs in <project> use it` is now written from the final reply's `report.applied` and `report.project`, and `--dir` is only a fallback for a daemon that does not send it. Test: `applied_names_the_repository_from_a_subdirectory` (RED: it named `<repo>/sub`).
  - **Review m3: a busy answer never hides a write.** `stats_with_tuning` takes `(repo_dir, project)` and a `StatsAsk { apply, dismiss, read_only }`, and `stats_within` adds a test's bound. The blocking work asks a shared `AtomicU8` to move from running to committing just before its only write. At the deadline, the async side moves it from running to abandoned. If that succeeds, the answer is `TUNING_STATS_BUSY` (now `the tuning file stayed busy (a run is starting); nothing was applied or dismissed; try again`) and nothing is written. If the write had already begun, its own result is awaited and returned (`JoinHandle`, never a blocking wait). Test: `a_busy_stats_answer_never_hides_a_write` runs 40 times at a bound of 0, plus once with the lock held, and checks that the answer matches the file. With the commit gate removed it fails: `answered busy, yet the threshold was written`.
  - **Review m4.** A refused request (an unknown id) still records reverts first. Every `run stats` does that, and reverts must be recorded before the proposals are derived, so it stays. What a refusal leaves untouched is `tuning.toml`. Test: `a_refused_request_writes_no_tuning_file` sends a raw request with a known and an unknown id on a fresh seed. It pins existing behaviour and passed before the fix.
  - **Review m5.** The opening pins are exact again:
    - `app_tests/settings_screen.rs::opened` and `c_b_s_opens_from_the_cache` assert that the one effect is the tagged read-only `Stats` for `goal_project()` (`only_the_tuning_ask`).
    - `opening_settings_sends_a_read_only_stats` asserts a single effect, and no effect at all with no project or with the screen already open.
  - **Review m6.** A save can make a budget explicit, so on the screen's own `Saved` reply the screen asks the read-only `Stats` again (`screens.rs`, `settings_tuning_ask`, now `pub(in crate::app)`). Each ask clears the drawn notes until its answer comes. Test: `a_save_asks_the_tuning_again`, which fails with the re-ask removed. `settings_screen_save.rs::w_sends_put_and_saved_shows_the_line` now expects that one effect.
  - **Review m7.** `run_cmd/adapt.rs` is over its bound (now 374): accepted as recorded.

### Task M9.5.12 (2026-10-04): critical-path weights and the run estimate

Decisions 7 (engine half), 14 and 15 and rulings RE-1 to RE-4 as written, with these readings:

- **`model.rs` was split first** (controller addendum; its own move-only commit, 3513 daemon tests before and after): `Run`'s lookups and path helpers (`same_on_disk` … `full_path`) and the free `task_branch` and `task_path` moved to `run/model_paths.rs` (101), mounted `#[path] mod paths; pub use paths::{task_branch, task_path};`. `model.rs` 477→390, then +13 for decision 15's fields: 403.
- **`schedule::weight(limits, task) -> u64`**: a hub task takes `hub_secs` and an L task `m_secs` when `path_weights` is `Some`. Without weights it keeps today's rule exactly (S 1, M and L 3, by size), so `Tuned::default()` reproduces today; decision 7's "3 (… hub)" is not applied to an S hub task, which today weighs 1. `critical_lens` returns `Vec<u64>` and adds with `saturating_add`.
- **Decision 15's fields** are `#[serde(default)]` and skipped while at their defaults (`Option::is_none`, a private `model::is_zero`), so `model_tuning_tests::an_old_run_json_still_loads` (9.3's `run.json` written back byte-equal) holds. `Round.approved_at` likewise.
- **Where the reducer does it.** `engine/mod.rs::finish`, for each run that changed (the condition that bumps its revision), calls `paused(old.state, run, now)`: entering `paused` or `halted` sets `paused_at`, leaving them adds the span to `paused_secs`, and every change sets `last_step_at`. It runs after the urgent/counter comparison, so a counter-only change stays a counter change. A run new to the state (a start, a restore) is left as it arrived, as its revision is.
- **"paused_secs since the approval".** Decision 14 subtracts the paused time since the round's approval. `paused_secs` is reset to 0 by `goal_rounds_end::approved` (every round's approval), so it always holds exactly that; no further field is needed. A run cannot be approved while paused.
- **The round's approval time** is set in `goal_rounds_end::approved` (the one function both approval paths call: `requests::approve` and an orchestrator round's `--yes` submit in `orch.rs`), for round 2 and later; round 1's stays `Run.approved_at`. `engine/requests.rs` is therefore not touched.
- **Restore** (`engine/restore.rs`): after `settle`, a run the restore turned `paused` gets `paused_at = Run.restored`, else `last_step_at`, else (a run from before 9.5, `last_step_at` 0) the restore's `now`.
- **Concern (RE-4 against the code):** `Run.restored` is set by `settle` to the restore's own `now` (`restore.rs:276`, whenever a task has a round), not to the old daemon's end. So "`paused_at = run.restored`" starts the pause at the restart, and the downtime before it counts as elapsed work; only the `last_step_at` branch (a run with no session yet) counts the downtime as paused, as decision 15's "so the downtime counts as paused" intends. Implemented and tested as written (`a_restart_counts_downtime_as_paused`: restored 5000, resume 9100, 4100). If the intent is to count the downtime, the fix is one line: prefer `last_step_at` (a lower bound of the old daemon's end) over `restored`; it needs a ruling.
- **`estimate.rs` readings.**
  - "Running" for RE-2's `None` is a task that is not finished, `pending` or `blocked` (queued, preparing, working, in a gate or the merge queue).
  - `done(t)`'s open phase counts only up to `paused_at` while the run is paused, so neither `elapsed` nor `done` grows during a pause (`paused_time_is_not_work`). After a resume the open phase includes the pause, as `phases` do (Risk 1; task clocks are unchanged by decision 15).
  - A round that has ended (`Round.ended_at`) stops the estimate's clock there, so a finished round's ratio stays what it ended at instead of rising forever.
  - `pr` mode subtracts, for the current round's stages (`Run::round_of_stage`), `review_wait_secs` plus the wait in progress (`now − wait_from`), which `StagePrInfo.human_review_secs` folds in only at the next delivery pass.
  - `bound_ratio_permille` is `None` only when it does not fit a `u32`.
- **Tests.** Pure ones in `run/estimate_tests.rs` (248); the reducer's in `engine/tests/estimate.rs` (169): `weights_change_dispatch_order`, `paused_time_is_not_work`, `a_restart_counts_downtime_as_paused`, `tick_without_a_due_change_still_changes_nothing`, and the extra `a_later_rounds_approval_is_recorded` (the engine half of RE-1). `ratio_is_none_before_approval` is pinning (the fields were always `None`). `tick_without_a_due_change_still_changes_nothing` also asserts `last_step_at` is the last changing step's time, which failed before. `engine/tests/orch_edit.rs::but_the_log` zeroes `last_step_at` beside `revision`: a logged, rejected batch is a change.
- **Sizes.** `estimate.rs` 193 (new), `engine/mod.rs` 408→434 (+26 of +30), `engine/schedule.rs` 346→351, `engine/restore.rs` 521→527, `engine/goal_rounds_end.rs` 264→269, `snapshot.rs` 474→476, `plan.rs` 569→572, `model_goal_rounds.rs` 115→120.
- **Fix round 1 (rulings T12-1, T12-2, T12-3; review m1–m7).** This supersedes the "paused_secs since the approval", Restore, Concern and `done` open-phase bullets above.
  - **T12-1.** On restore, `paused_at = last_step_at`, else the restore's `now`; `Run.restored` is no longer read for it, so the daemon's downtime is paused time. `a_restart_counts_downtime_as_paused` is re-pinned: `paused_at == last_step_at` and `paused_secs == 9100 − last`.
  - **m1.** The same rule (`engine/pause.rs::restored`) applies to every run the restore leaves `paused` or `halted` with no `paused_at`: one the restart paused, one a replayed result halted, and one stored before 9.5 (its `last_step_at` is 0, so from the restore). Test `a_run_restored_stopped_is_paused_from_its_last_change` (halted on disk: from its last change; a 9.3-style paused run: from the restore, 4100 s at a 9100 resume).
  - **`paused_secs` is cumulative** again (decision 15 as written; no reset at approval). `Round.paused_before` (`#[serde(default)]`, skipped at 0, like `windows_before`) records it at each approval, and `elapsed` takes off `Run::paused_total(end) − paused_before`. `Run::paused_total(at)` (`model_paths.rs`) is `paused_secs` plus the span in progress.
  - **T12-2.** `Task.paused: TaskPaused { base, active }` (`model_tuning.rs`; one `#[serde(default)]` field, skipped at zero): `base` is the run's paused total when the current phase began, `active` the paused seconds inside its earlier active phases. The reducer keeps them in `engine/pause.rs::account` (called from `finish` for each changed run, beside the pause accounting moved there from `engine/mod.rs`): a task whose `phase_since` changed in the step adds `total − base` to `active` when the phase it left was active, and sets `base = total`. `done(t)` = `active_secs(phases) − paused.active` + `(end − phase_since) − (paused_total(end) − paused.base)`. A pause therefore never counts as work, open or closed, and the open phase needs no clamp. `paused_time_is_not_work` pauses and resumes without touching `phase_since` (2101 throughout): `left` is 5600 at the pause and 5100 at 5001, not 3100; the claim that ends the phase moves 2000 paused seconds into `active`.
  - **T12-3.** No counted task (a round whose tasks were all cancelled): no estimate. Test `no_counted_task_no_estimate`.
  - **m4.** `pr` mode: the open review wait counts only up to a pause, so it is not taken off twice with the paused time. Recorded readings: earlier rounds' stages are not subtracted (their `review_wait_secs` holds time before this round's approval, and no split is recorded); a closed review wait that overlapped an earlier pause can still be taken off twice (saturating, so `elapsed` never goes below 0).
  - **m5** (S hub weight 1 without weights) and **m6** (`ratio_is_none_before_approval` pinning) are accepted by the controller. **m7.** `longest` maps ids to indices once (`HashMap`), so it is linear in edges.
  - Tests: `paused_time_is_not_work` (rewritten), `a_restart_counts_downtime_as_paused` (re-pinned), `a_run_restored_stopped_is_paused_from_its_last_change`, `no_counted_task_no_estimate`, and in `a_later_round_is_estimated_from_its_own_approval` the time paused since round 2's approval only (1066). All five failed against 3b405d37.
  - Sizes: `estimate.rs` 196, `engine/pause.rs` 71 (new, pure), `engine/mod.rs` 437→421, `engine/restore.rs` 523, `engine/goal_rounds_end.rs` 271, `model_tuning.rs` 312→330, `model_task.rs` 231, `model_paths.rs` 108, `model_goal_rounds.rs` 124, `validate.rs` 538→539 (one literal line; it was already past its budget).

### Task M9.5.13 (2026-10-04): adaptive concurrency

Decision 16 and rulings RC-1, RC-3 as written, with the controller addenda (task M9.5.3b review m2, ruling T9-2) and these readings:

- **Settings frozen at start** (ruling T9-2). `RunLimits` gains `adaptive_concurrency`, `recover_after_secs` (`recover_after_mins × 60`) and `halve_hold_secs`, copied from `[orchestrator.tuning]` by `plan::run_limits`, the one place `build_run` takes the config's limits. Each is `#[serde(default)]` and skipped at `false`/0, so a 9.3 `run.json` reads as off (and writes back byte-equal, `an_old_run_json_still_loads`); `off_means_today` loads `m93-run.json` and checks that nothing changes it. `race_slot_wait_secs` is the race's (M9.5.17a), not added here.
- **No `Default` for `RuntimeConcurrency`** (3b review m2): an entry is made by `RuntimeConcurrency::new(max_writers)` at a runtime's first event; `a_runtime_first_seen_mid_run_starts_at_max_writers` covers a second runtime first seen after the first was halved.
- **What changes a cap.** `count_rate_limit(run, i, r, now)` (it gained `now`; four callers) counts as before and calls `concurrency::on_rate_limit`. A halving that would not lower the cap (already 1) is not a change: no log line, `halvings` and `last_change_at` unchanged, only `last_rate_limit_at` moves. So `a_rate_limit_halves_the_runtimes_cap_never_below_one`'s four events give 3 halvings.
- **Recovery** runs on `EventKind::Tick` before the step's scheduler pass, so a recovered slot is used in the same step. It is skipped for a terminal run (accepted, discarded, failed), which dispatches nothing more; a `complete` run keeps recovering, because 9.3 can start a round on it. The log line's minutes are `recover_after_secs / 60`.
- **Attention and `writer_caps`.** `writer_caps` lists every runtime below `max_writers` (empty while off). The attention line is left out on a terminal run (`an_ended_run_keeps_its_caps_quietly`). The TUI's alerts read typed alerts only, never `attention` (`tui/src/app/alerts.rs:265`), so nothing there changes (RC-3); no TUI file is touched (the `agents` row's `(writers <cap>/<max>)` is task M9.5.20's).
- **`## Concurrency`** (no text was given): rendered only when a rate limit reached a runtime with adaptive concurrency on, before `## Log`, one line per runtime: `- codex: writers 1 of 3 (2 rate limits, 1 halving, 0 recoveries)`. The cap changes themselves are in `## Log`.
- **Lanes** (RR-9, ready for M9.5.17a): `schedule::writer_slots(task)` returns the runtime of each writer slot a task holds: a race with no winner yet holds one per lane in `preparing`, `working`, `proof` or `check` (the phases `holds_writer` gives a task), each on its lane's route; otherwise one on `task.route` while `holds_writer`. `writers_busy` is its sum and `writers_busy_on` its count per runtime. `writer_slots_count_live_lanes_on_their_runtimes` pins it (with the branch removed it fails 1 against 2); 17a may refine which lane states count.
- **Dispatch.** In `dispatch_writers`, after the `max_writers` and hub `break`, a task whose route's runtime has no room (`concurrency::has_room`) is skipped with `continue`, so a later task on another runtime still starts.
- **Pinning tests.** `off_means_today`'s old-run and off halves pass on the stub (they pin today's behaviour); its third part (the same run with it on holds `t3`) failed on the stub. `a_run_without_a_rate_limit_has_no_concurrency_section` is pinning. Every other test failed on a stub `concurrency.rs` whose functions changed nothing.
- **Extra tests:** `a_runtime_first_seen_mid_run_starts_at_max_writers`, `writer_slots_count_live_lanes_on_their_runtimes`, `an_ended_run_keeps_its_caps_quietly`, `a_run_without_a_rate_limit_has_no_concurrency_section`.
- **Sizes.** `engine/concurrency.rs` 147 (new), `engine/tests/concurrency.rs` 523 (new), `engine/signals.rs` 567→570, `engine/dispatch_launch.rs` 265→269, `engine/schedule.rs` 351→375, `engine/mod.rs` 434→437, `snapshot.rs` 476→479, `report.rs` 320→322, `plan.rs` 572→576, `model_limits.rs` 158→176, `model_tuning.rs` 297→312.

### Task M9.5.14 (2026-10-04): `race` and `pair`, plan fields and validation

Decisions 17, 24 and 32 and rulings RR-5 to RR-8 (review ruling I9) as written, with the controller addenda and these readings:

- **Where the rules run.** `run/validate_patterns.rs::validate` is called once at the end of `validate_graph::validate_tasks_with`, after every task's own resolution, so `pair`'s tdd check sees the resolved test mode (rule 8.3 applied). Like the L rule, it checks only unfinished `touched` tasks, so an edit elsewhere never re-judges an existing race. `validate.rs` takes no call (it loses the interim refusal, 539 → 533).
- **The second runtime's source** (interface deviation). `validate_tasks_with` had neither roster nor what is installed, so it and `validate_tasks` gain a last parameter `peers: Peers<'_>` = `(&[ModelEntry], &Installed)`. `Installed` is `route_pick::Installed` (`BTreeMap<String, bool>`, the run's own `run.orch.installed`), not the Interfaces' `BTreeMap<Runtime, bool>`. Callers: `build_run` passes `config.models` and an empty map (what is installed is recorded on the run after the build, `make_planned`; empty means every runtime counts as installed, as for a plan file); `apply_edits` and `engine/fixes.rs` pass the run's roster and `run.orch.installed`. `validate_tests_fields.rs` shadows `validate_tasks` with a local helper over the default roster, so its twelve call sites are unchanged.
- **The race rules, in order:** not a code task, else a hub, else no peer; one race error per task. The exclusive rule (`pair: a task cannot both race and pair`) is reported beside it. **"The installed and model-list skips of decision 9a"** reads as: the peer is `roster::first_at(roster, peer(runtime), strength)` on a runtime not recorded `false`; "failed in this task" cannot apply before dispatch, and no class list is consulted (decision 19 names the roster's peer). `peer_route` lives in `validate_patterns.rs`, not the Interfaces' `role_launch_patterns.rs` (task M9.5.18's new file), which should reuse it.
- **"Started"** (`validate_patterns::has_started`) is the stage rule's code exactly: not `not_started`, or a start commit. A pending or queued task with a start commit therefore counts as started too, as it does for `stage`.
