The refusal runs in `edits_amend.rs` after the finished-task check and before the restricted fields, one sentence per field named, whatever the value (clearing included, task 2 review m5).
- **RR-7 needed no code**: `apply_edits` already runs `validate_rounds::earlier_round` before any edit. `an_earlier_rounds_task_is_refused_first` is a pinning test and passed before the change.
- **RR-6, gate holds: no code.** `gate_holds::assign` holds every orchestrator *addition* under an open epic's round or the open promotion, whatever its fields, so a racing or paired `add_task` is held exactly like any other (`an_orchestrators_race_goes_through_a_gate_hold`). M9 holds no *amend*: an amend keeps the task's existing hold (`amending_a_held_task_keeps_its_hold`), and a released task stays released. A `race`/`pair` amend is treated the same ("like any orchestrator task change"); `edits.rs` takes no hold code. **Ruling T14-1** (controller, 2026-10-04): no new hold machinery; additions are held under an open epic or promotion; an amend keeps the task's existing hold; a race/pair amend on a released task that was never dispatched goes through unheld, and the project-trust check (`edits_may_widen`) covers it.
- **RR-6, reach.** `reachable_runtimes` adds `peer_route` (lane b, and the test writer when it has one) to each racing or paired task's starting routes. It changes no result today: decision 39's escalation from the task's route already reaches the peer runtime's first entry at the same strength, which is a superset of `peer_route` (which also skips what is not installed). `edits_may_widen` already counts every `amend_task`. So `race_and_pair_widen_reach` and the engine's trust test pin the property; their RED was the interim refusal, not the reach code. Neither test can detect the reach code being lost today, because escalation already reaches the peer (review m3, accepted).
- **The daemon's bounds table** (`orch/tools_bounds.rs`, not in the file list) refused `race` and `pair` as `unknown field` before serde ever saw them; both are added as `Rule::Serde` to `EDIT` and `TASK`. A `race: "yes"` is then refused by serde (`expected a boolean`), tested through `edit_plan`.
- **The MCP schema test** has no validator crate to lean on; `tools_orch_tests.rs::conforms` checks the plain schemas these tools use (`type`, `enum`, `required`, closed `properties`, `items`).
- **Digest and `task_result`.** `digest::race_text` / `pair_text` (`pub(super)`, reused by `result.rs`) give `a claude working · b codex review` (lane, runtime label, the lane state's serde label), `won by <lane>` once a winner is set (adopted too), `writing the test`, `implementing, red <sha7>` (`implementing` while `red` is unset), else `null`. Both keys follow `route` in the task entry; `digest_fixture.json` gains them as `null`. `digest_trim.rs` is unchanged: `drop_task` removes the whole entry, which `a_trimmed_task_takes_its_race_and_pair_with_it` pins.
- **The dispatch gate** (addendum). `validate_patterns::PATTERNS_DISPATCH` (false) is the one gate: nothing in dispatch reads `race` or `pair`, so a validated racing or paired task starts one ordinary `Worker` on its own route and checkout (`race_and_pair_tasks_dispatch_as_ordinary_workers_for_now`, which also asserts the constant). Tasks M9.5.16 and M9.5.17a branch on it and set it to true.
- **Removed:** M9.5.2's `not available yet` refusal in `validate::check_fields` and its two tests (`race_and_pair_are_not_available_yet`, `amend_race_or_pair_is_not_available_yet`).
- **Sizes** (from `ca251667`): `validate_patterns.rs` 136 and `validate_patterns_tests.rs` 295 (new), `validate.rs` 539→533, `validate_graph.rs` 440→449 (the table's +6 was 436-based and task M9.5.10a used 4; this task +9), `edits.rs` 440→441, `edits_amend.rs` 217→224, `reach.rs` 114→122, `plan.rs` 576→578 (already past the table's 573), `orch/contract.rs` 511 (appended on the same lines), `orch/digest.rs` 401→431 (+30 of +20: the two text functions and the new test module's registration), `orch/digest_trim.rs` 216 (unchanged), `orch/result.rs` 251→253, `orch/tools_bounds.rs` 189→195, `engine/fixes.rs` 152→153, `run/mod.rs` 83→84, `mcp/src/tools_orch.rs` 301→306. New test files: `orch/digest_tests_patterns.rs` 163, `engine/tests/race_pair_plan.rs` 151.
- **Fix round 1 (review I1, m1, m2, m3, m4).** The race and pair rules now judge only touched tasks that have not started (`has_started` false): a started task's race and pair were decided at dispatch (RR-8), and rung 2 or a substitution may since have moved its route where no peer exists, which made every later brief or acceptance amend fail (the A-I1 class; `a_started_racing_task_on_a_moved_route_still_takes_a_brief`). An amend naming `race` or `pair` and a restricted field on a started task reports both refusals in one reply (`a_started_tasks_race_and_route_amend_reports_both_refusals`). `has_started` moved to `edits_state.rs`, and the stage refusal (ruling C-14(d)) calls it too, so I9's one predicate has one copy. ### Task M9.5.15 (2026-10-04): lane checkouts, the crown, stale locks, `keep_head` and the red-only proof

Decisions 19 (checkouts), 21, 22 (git half), 25 (the red-only proof) and 27, rulings RR-1, RR-2, RP-1, T1-2 and T1-3, and review ruling I2, as written, with these readings:

- **`Task::checkout_name`** returns `<task>.<lane>` for the lane whose state is `Won` or `Adopted` (the free `model::lane_checkout(task, lane)` spells it), else the task id. Every site that derived one of the task's checkouts from `task.id()` now goes through it: `role_launch.rs` (the worker's `TMPDIR`, sandbox roots and filter log dir; the reviewer's checkout), the four places a task's `worktree` is first set (`plan.rs`, `edits.rs`, `engine/fixes.rs`, `engine/integration.rs`, where it is always the id), and, beyond the file list, the engine's proof, check and tier-1 scratch checkout (`gates.rs`, `tiers.rs`), the review checkout (`review.rs`, twice; `kinds.rs`'s review task, which never races), the merge's clean-up (`merge.rs`) and the run's accept or discard list (`complete.rs::run_worktrees`). That list also carries every other lane checkout of a race with its `.review` and `.proof`, so a loser's kept checkout goes with the run's other checkouts at accept or discard (decision 22). A gate reject's discard list (`requests.rs`) is unchanged: nothing has raced at the gate. The driver's `worker_git_dirs` takes the session's lane from `RunRef.lane` (`<task>.<lane>`), else the task's `checkout_name`; its role filter is still `Worker` only (racers and test writers are task M9.5.18's).
- **The crown** (`git/crown.rs`): `update-ref --no-deref refs/heads/<task_branch> <lane_head> <zero oid>` (`--no-deref` added, as every other engine `update-ref`), the zero oid read first with `rev-parse --show-object-format` (so `recording_git` shows that read, the `update-ref`, then the `rev-parse --verify` of ruling T1-2). A `lane_head` that is not a commit id is an error. A failed create whose branch is at the lane head but symbolic is `RefMoved`, as reconcile treats a symbolic stage branch.
- **The executor** (`driver/lane_ops.rs`): `crown_racer` runs the crown and, on `Crowned`, `repin_own` inside the same queued write, on its blocking thread (the re-pin canonicalises the path). `remove_lane` executes every `RemoveWorktree` (with no flag it is decision 20's salvage and removal, unchanged), so `driver/ops.rs` has one arm per op; `driver/cleanup.rs` splits its removal into `salvage_in` and `remove_keeping` for it. The lock clearing runs on a blocking thread before the salvage, only when the checkout still exists. `clear_stale_locks` refuses a git directory that is not a directory, removes a lock that is a link without following it, and leaves a directory under a lock's name. A partial commit's `next-index-<pid>.lock` (M9.5.1 item 8) is left, as Risk 8 says, and the test pins that.
- **`salvage`** gains `keep_head` as its fifth argument (Interfaces); every existing caller (two in `src`, nine test files) passes `false`. With it, a clean checkout's ref is created at its head (the imported head of a task checkout); a ref already there is the salvage replayed, one elsewhere an error.
- **The red-only proof** is `ProofOp.red_only` (a field, set from `OpKind::Proof.red_only`); the head run is skipped when it is set. No engine site sets it yet (task M9.5.16).
- **Serialisation.** `OpKind::Proof.red_only`, `RemoveWorktree.{keep_head, clear_locks, keep_path}` and `OpResult::Removed.cleared_locks` are skipped while unset, so a journal line written before them is written back unchanged. `CrownRacer` and `Crowned` are appended last.
- **Reconcile.** `CrownRacer` and `CreateStageBranch` share one row, `reconcile/git.rs::created_at` (the stage branch's, with the result to replay as a parameter; its texts are unchanged); `reconcile/mod.rs` (not in the file list) gains the arm. `RemoveWorktree` with `keep_path` and its checkout still there is `Removed` when the salvage ref exists, else not started; with `keep_head` alone it is the existing row. A replayed `Removed` has no `cleared_locks` (they are not known any more).
- **A lost crown** (reconcile: not started) is sent again under a new id by `engine/restore.rs::lost`, with the other idempotent git ops (one line, not in the file list; `a_lost_crown_is_sent_again`), since decision 27 says the op re-runs.
- **The restart re-pin** (`run_worktree_paths`): a task's own checkout is pinned at `run.task_path(checkout_name)` once a lane is crowned, not at `task.worktree`, so a restart between a lane's `Won` and the crown's result (task M9.5.17a moves `task.worktree` there) still pins it, under the task branch; the re-run crown's compare-and-swap then makes the branch. No import runs in that window: a lane that passed has no op left but the crown. Every other lane checkout is pinned under its own branch, each with its `.review` and `.proof` read-only.
- **Tests.** Part 1 is split: `run/checkout_name_tests.rs` (`checkout_name_is_the_task_until_a_lane_is_crowned`, `every_launch_site_follows_checkout_name`, the pinning `an_unraced_task_keeps_its_own_paths`) and `engine/tests/checkout_name.rs` (`every_gate_checkout_follows_checkout_name`, `tier_1_runs_in_the_lanes_proof_checkout`, `the_merge_removes_the_lanes_checkouts`, `the_runs_clean_up_lists_every_lane_checkout`, the pinning `an_unraced_tasks_clean_up_is_unchanged`, `a_lost_crown_is_sent_again`). The executor tests are `driver/lane_ops_tests.rs` (`ops::run` is the driver's): `after_the_crown_imports_move_the_task_branch` (with the re-pin removed it fails, the own branch staying the lane's), `a_moved_branch_is_not_repinned`, `keep_path_salvages_and_leaves_the_checkout`, `a_lanes_removal_clears_its_stale_locks_first`. The restart test is `driver/restore_lanes_tests.rs`. `daemon/tests/run_git_crown.rs` has the git ones, with `a_linked_lock_is_removed_never_followed`; the red-only proof is in `tests/run_proof_slots.rs` (its slot rig); the reconcile rows in `tests/run_journal/git_crown.rs`. `run/test_support.rs::race_of` builds a race for them.
- **Fix round 1** (review of `fea03a4d`): with `keep_path` no lock is cleared, even with `clear_locks` (ruling RR-2: the racer did not exit, so a lock may be a live process's); the clearing is the first write in the repository's git queue; a removal that fails after clearing names the cleared locks in its failure (`… (after it removed a stale index.lock left by the stopped racer, …)`); `engine/restore.rs::lost` keeps a re-sent op's `PendingOp.lane` (`emit_op` records none); `Task::checkout_name` and the driver's `checkout_of` read the stored `Lane.checkout`, and `model::lane_checkout` is only the name a `Lane` is made with. `git/checkout.rs` and `Run::task_path` needed no change (the path helpers are keyed by name), so `a_lane_checkout_has_its_own_repository` passed before the change: it pins that. Tests added: `a_failed_removal_still_names_the_locks_it_cleared`, `the_locks_are_cleared_inside_the_write_queue`, `checkout_name_is_the_stored_lanes_checkout`, `a_racers_checkout_is_its_lanes_stored_one`; `keep_path_salvages_and_leaves_the_checkout` now passes `clear_locks: true` and checks both locks remain; `a_lost_crown_is_sent_again` checks the lane.
- **Sizes** (from `8ebbf5f8`): `git/crown.rs` 118, `driver/lane_ops.rs` 106 (new); `driver/ops.rs` 541→546, `worktree/pinned.rs` 439→450, `git/salvage.rs` 387→424 (+37 of +25: `keep_at_head` with its replay check), `git/mod.rs` 570→572, `proof.rs` 283→287, `reconcile/git.rs` 544→560, `reconcile/mod.rs` 439→453, `driver/restore.rs` 484→502, `driver/cleanup.rs` 174→197, `engine/ops.rs` 430→462, `engine/op_result.rs` 187→196, `model_task.rs` 231→256, `engine/complete.rs` 496→505. The `Removed { salvage_ref: None }` literals of 13 engine test files gained `cleared_locks` (rustfmt spreads each over four lines): `engine/tests/history.rs` is at 598, `merge.rs` 592, `delivery_land.rs` 591.

### Task M9.5.16 (2026-10-04): the test writer, then the implementer, in the reducer

Decisions 24–26 and rulings RP-1 and RP-2 as written, with ruling T14-1 (no new hold machinery) and RR-2 (no kill code: the writer is retired through `Effect::RetireWindow`, rung 2 and `run retry` stop it through the existing `KillWindow`), and these readings:

- **The dispatch gate.** Task M9.5.14's `PATTERNS_DISPATCH` gated nothing in dispatch; its pair half is open now and the constant is renamed `validate_patterns::RACE_DISPATCH` (still false), which task M9.5.17a flips. `race_tasks_dispatch_as_ordinary_workers_for_now` (renamed from `race_and_pair_tasks_…`) pins a racing task as an ordinary worker and a paired one as a test writer.
- **Where the pair lives.** `engine/pair.rs` (pure): `begin` at dispatch (`Pair { phase: Writing, writer_route }`, the route from M9.5.14's `validate_patterns::peer_route` over `run.orch.installed`, else the task's route; a task that already has a pair keeps it, so a retry that dispatches it again resumes its phase), `next_session` (the role and route of the next writing session), `escalate_writer`, `writer_rejection`, `implementer_mismatch`, `fill`, `on_red_checked`. Interface deviation: there is no `start_writer` or `on_writer_done`. The launch is `dispatch_launch::launch`, which takes the role and route from `pair::next_session` (`<h4>/<task>.t<n>`, `test_writer_spec`, the writer's route, no scout extract, recorded messages left for the implementer's notes); `launch_worker` and `launch_fresh` give a test writer its prompt, `launch_implementer` is new. A writer's accepted claim goes through decision 32's `accept` unchanged (a paired task is tdd, so its first gate is `proof`); `done_checked` only replaces the claim's red with the full head, and `gates::start_proof` sets `red_only` while the task is writing.
- **"Red must be the head."** A red that is a prefix of the claim's head of at least four hex digits (git's shortest abbreviation) is the head; the claim then carries the full sha, which the red-only proof and `pair.red` use. The check sits in `done::rejection` after the tdd rule (no counting, as every rejection). A writer's turn-end fallback claim, which names neither, gets the tdd rejection, not a proof (the tdd rule applies to a writer's claim however it came). A claim with no red reads `red is none`.
- **The implementer's claim.** A named test must equal the writer's; a named red must be a prefix (four or more digits) of `pair.red`, so a short sha is accepted as for the writer. The refusal is answered at the tool call, before `VerifyDone`. Fields left out are filled from the pair in `done::claim`, so the turn-end fallback's claim gets them too.
- **After the red check.** Besides decision 26's counters (`failures` into `pair.writer_failures`; `failures`, `bounces`, `stalls`, `budget_exceeded` to 0), `rung` goes to 0, the writer's `done` and `head` are cleared (an override of a blocked implementer must not see the writer's head as an accepted claim), and a new budget epoch starts (ruling T15-C1), so rung 4 weighs the implementer alone. The writer's `failure_log` stays: a fresh implementer's hand-over lists it under "Earlier failures". The log line is decision 25's, in the run log and the task's history.
- **Rung 2 and `run retry` while writing** escalate the writer's route (`roster::escalate`, as rung 2 escalates a worker's) and leave the task's route, which is the implementer's; no `escalated_from` or list step is recorded for it, and `every_route_failed` is not consulted. A fresh writer's first turn is `contract_patterns::test_writer_handover`: the writer's prompt and decision 30's hand-over part, now `contract::handover_tail` (extracted from `handover_prompt`, unchanged text).
- **RP-2 needs the driver.** The signals are read by `VerifyDone`'s executor over `<merge-base of run head and head>..<head>`, so "diffed from red" is a new `SignalsSpec.from` (`#[serde(default)]`, skipped while unset), set by `weakening::spec(run, task)` to `pair.red` for an implementer, and read by `driver/ops.rs::verify_done` through the existing `git::done_signals_from` (ruling C-21's sync read). The driver half is `driver/ops_signals_tests_pair.rs` on real git: from red the dropped assertion is an `AssertionLoss`; from the stage head, none.
- **The reviewer note** follows the signals block, whenever the implementer has a red (one sentence, decision 26). The implementer's note is the last section before the brief, in `worker_prompt` itself, so a fresh implementer's hand-over carries it too.
- **"Worker" checks that now see a test writer** (beyond the file list; the full audit is task M9.5.17a's): `model::writes_task(role)` (worker or test writer) is used by `ladder::{worker_round, kill_worker, supersede, fresh_due}`, `ladder_budget::total_spend`, `clock::{restart, epoch_spend}`, `signals.rs` (stream events, the turn end, the exit), `outbox.rs` (queue, target, delivery failures), `early.rs` (held tool calls), `restore.rs::relaunch`, `dispatch.rs` (a launched window makes the task `working`), `done.rs` and `worker_messages.rs` (the caller's role must be its round's), `engine/orch.rs` and `driver/orch.rs` (a test writer's `task_note`), `edits_state::has_live_worker`, `actions/effects.rs`, `rounds.rs::note_said`. Left worker-only: `history.rs` (`worker_usage`), `snapshot.rs`, `snapshot_detail.rs` (tasks M9.5.20, M9.5.21), `driver/ops.rs::worker_git_dirs` (task M9.5.18's addendum), `review.rs:164` (the reviewer's author is the implementer).
- **Routing history.** A test writer's launch records a `RoutingDecision` with role `test_writer`, trigger `test_writer`, source `class_default`, policy `m8a-worker-v1` (`routing::record_test_writer`; decision 9a names only the trigger). The implementer's first session still records `initial`.
- **Extra tests:** `a_restart_resumes_the_test_writer` (its relaunch half failed without the `restore.rs` arm; its resume half is pinning), and the routing record in `a_paired_task_starts_with_a_test_writer_on_the_peer_runtime`.
- **Sizes** (from `bd33aa89`): `engine/pair.rs` 179, `contract_patterns.rs` 150, `role_launch_patterns.rs` 26 (new; the racer half is task M9.5.18's); `engine/dispatch_launch.rs` 269→309 (+40, the table's whole +40), `engine/ladder.rs` 449→452 (+3 of +12), `run/contract.rs` 553→573 (+20 of +8: `handover_tail`'s extraction and the two note calls), `engine/weakening.rs` 247→254, `engine/gates.rs` 455→465, `engine/done.rs` 329→340, `engine/done_checked.rs` 245→250, `engine/requests.rs` 425→436, `routing.rs` 382→397, `driver/ops.rs` 546→550, `tiers/spec.rs` 130→135, `model_rounds.rs` 282→289. Tests: `engine/tests/pair.rs` 465, `engine/tests/pair_implementer.rs` 293, `driver/ops_signals_tests_pair.rs` 71 (new).

**Fix round 1 (2026-10-04), rulings T16-1 to T16-6 and the minors.** These amend the readings above where they differ.

- **T16-1 (amends RP-2).** `SignalsSpec.from` is now `SignalsSpec.red`. With a red set, `driver/ops.rs::verify_done` calls the new `git::pair_signals(git, worktree, (start, red, head), spec, t)` (`git/signals.rs`, through `Git`, so `--no-optional-locks` and the scrubbed environment hold; it runs in the existing `VerifyDone` executor, never under the manager lock). It finds the newest first-parent merge in `red..head` (`rev-list --first-parent --merges -n 1`). Without one, it reads `red..head` as before. With one, it reads `<merge>..head`, drops that read's signals on the paths `start..red` touched, and adds `red..head`'s signals on those paths only. Deleted test files and `DiffTooLarge` come first; the list is capped at `SIGNALS_MAX`; `more` adds both reads' overflow, so it may overcount by a dropped red-path signal; `base` is the merge. Real-git test: `a_merge_after_red_is_not_the_implementers_work` (a run-head commit deleting another test file, merged `--no-ff` after the implementer's commit).
- **T16-2.** `route_pick::writer_route(run, i)` is `peer_route` over `open_roster(run, i, true)` (installed and overlap skips), else the task's route; `begin` uses it. `route_pick::writer_step(run, i, current)` is `roster_substitute` for a route that failed in this task, else `escalate_skipping` over the same open roster. `pair::escalate_writer(run, i, now)` (rung 2 and `run retry`) uses it, logs `every_route_failed`'s line, and records the route it stepped from in the new `Pair.escalated_from` (`#[serde(default)]`, skipped while unset). `roster::escalate` is no longer used for a writer.
- **T16-3.** `on_red_checked` does the hand-over but no longer launches. The new `pair::launch_due`, called first in `dispatch::schedule`'s running branch, launches the implementer of a handed-over task that is `working`, not held, without a fresh session, with no worker round and no `CreateWindow` in flight. A paused or halted run therefore launches it on resume. The writer is retired at the red check either way.
- **T16-4.** `merge.rs` retires every writing session (`model::writes`), a test writer's included; task 17a's `TestWriter` exclusion is removed.
- **T16-5.** `actions/rules.rs::override_refusal` first refuses a writing task with exactly `task <id> is still writing its test; override it once its implementer has started`. This covers `override_task` and `gates::override_counted`.
- **T16-6.** While the test is being written, nothing is merged into the writer's checkout:
  - `worker_messages::refresh_pass` skips a writing task, so its refresh stays `due` until the implementer's first turn boundary.
  - `holds_mail` does not hold a writer's mail, so the writer still hears its red check.
  - `holds::resume_held` lifts a writing task's hold without a hand-back (new `lift_writing`: an answered task resumes, and the run head is queued as a decision 42e refresh).
  - `hand_back::start_due_hand_backs` skips a writing task (defensive).
- **m1.** `routing::record_test_writer` records:
  - trigger `test_writer` once, with source `peer_route` (the peer pick), else `explicit_task` or `class_default` from the task's route;
  - `escalation` (`escalation_policy`, `m8a-escalate-v1`, the escalation pool marked with failed routes) after rung 2 or retry;
  - nothing for a same-route fresh session.
- **m2.** The implementer's red prefix is lower-cased before the comparison, as the writer's is.
- **m3.** Each red check pushes a `ProofRecord` with the new `red_only: true` (`#[serde(default)]`, skipped while false). `history::gates` (now `pub(crate)`) and the snapshot's `last_proof` leave such records out. The report labels them `Red check <n>`. The orch result JSON carries `red_only`, and its `ok` is `red_failed` for a red-only record.
- **m4, declined.** The red check does not require the test name in `red_tail`. For a compiled language, a compile failure at red is the normal TDD red, and it names no test. The full proof's red run does not match names either: only the head run does (`matched`). A test that matched nothing is caught by the implementer's full proof. This is a design note for the brief's owner.
- **m5.** `driver/orch.rs` routes the read tools by `!model::writes_task(role)`.
- **m6.** The Interfaces block's `engine/pair.rs` lines are amended to the code as built.
- **m7.** `the_test_writer_contract_matches_the_workers_lines` failed RED only through a bug in the test itself; it is a pinning test.
- **Tests** (`engine/tests/pair_fixes.rs`, new, 321 lines). All of these failed before the fix, `a_red_check_leaves_a_red_only_proof_record` by not compiling:
  - `the_writers_rung_2_skips_a_runtime_that_is_not_installed`, `a_writer_that_failed_in_this_task_is_substituted_on_retry`, `the_writers_dispatch_route_keeps_the_overlap_rule` (T16-2);
  - `the_implementer_waits_for_a_running_run` (T16-3);
  - `a_merge_retires_a_live_test_writer` (T16-4);
  - `override_is_refused_while_the_test_is_being_written` (T16-5);
  - `a_refresh_waits_for_the_implementer`, `a_held_writer_resumes_without_a_hand_back` (T16-6);
  - `a_red_check_leaves_a_red_only_proof_record` (m3).

  m1's source and same-route assertions were added to `a_paired_task_starts_with_a_test_writer_on_the_peer_runtime` and `a_restart_resumes_the_test_writer`.
- **Sizes** (from `36b86ccb`):

  | File | Lines |
  |------|-------|
  | `engine/pair.rs` | 179→224 |
  | `git/signals.rs` | 302→374 |
  | `routing.rs` | 397→429 |
  | `route_pick_step.rs` | 173→197 |
  | `engine/holds.rs` | 257→282 |
  | `engine/actions/rules.rs` | 431→438 |
  | `model_rounds.rs` | 307→312 |
  | `snapshot.rs` | 479→484 |
  | `model_tuning.rs` | 367→371 |
  | `engine/worker_messages.rs` | 327→330 |
  | `driver/ops.rs` | 550→552 |
  | `driver/ops_signals_tests_pair.rs` | 71→148 |

  Every other file changed by at most 2 lines. No file is over 600.

**Fix round 2 (2026-10-04), ruling T16-7 and the re-review's N2–N4.** Ruling T16-7 supersedes fix round 1's T16-1 bullet on the choice of base.

- **T16-7 (N1).** No merge commit is ever a signals base. `git::pair_signals(git, worktree, (start, red, run_head, head), spec, t)` no longer looks for merges. It makes two reads:
  - the paths `start..red` touched (the writer's) are read over `red..head`, those paths only;
  - every other path is read with `done_signals(run_head, head)`, the merge-base read the stage-head path uses. A run-head merge only moves that base to the run head, and the base never moves past the implementer's own commits, whoever made the merge.

  Two real-git tests in `driver/ops_signals_tests_pair.rs` failed before this change and pass after it. In both, the implementer weakens the writer's test and another test it owns, and both weakenings are caught:
  - `a_weakening_before_a_refresh_merge_is_caught`: an engine-style `--no-ff` refresh merge of the run head follows, and the run head's deletion of another test raises nothing;
  - `a_weakening_before_the_agents_own_merge_is_caught`: the implementer `--no-ff` merges an empty side commit itself.

  These replace fix round 1's `a_merge_after_red_is_not_the_implementers_work`. The rig gained `verify_at(run_head, …)`.
- **N2.** `ClaimSignals.base` is `red` when a caught `DeletedTestFile` is on a writer's path, so decision 41's restore command names a commit that has the file. Otherwise it is the merge base. One base serves the whole command, so a claim that deletes both a writer's test and a non-writer test that only the run head brought would restore the second from red, where it is absent. Test: `a_deleted_writers_test_is_restored_from_red`.

  - `pair::escalate_writer` logs `route_pick::writer_route_failed(run, i, current, next)`, so the wording's substitute is computed from the writer's route. No test can tell the two apart today: `roster_substitute` returns `Some` exactly when an unfailed entry is left, whatever route it starts from, so the change only makes the call say what it means.

**Fix round 3 (2026-10-04), ruling T16-8 and the second re-review's NB-1 to NB-3.**

- **Code layout.** `git::pair_signals` moved to the new `git/signals_pair.rs` (119 lines). `signals.rs` gained a pathspec argument on `unified_zero`, `signal_paths` and `read_signals`, plus a `merge_base` helper.
- **(a), NB-2: each read is limited by a pathspec.**
  - The `red..head` read covers only the writer's paths (`:(literal)<path>`).
  - The merge-base read from the run head excludes them (`:(exclude,literal)<path>`).
  - So each read's cap, cut and `DiffTooLarge` concern only its own paths, and `more` is exact. The run head's signals cannot push the writer-test weakening out of the cap.
  - Test: `run_head_signals_cannot_crowd_out_the_writers_test`. A refresh brings 21 deleted test files; the writer-test loss is still listed, with `more` 0. Before the fix: `([], 2)`.
- **(b), NB-1: each deleted file has its own restore base.**
  - New field `ClaimSignals.restore_from` (`#[serde(default)]`, a path→base map): `red` for a writer's path, the merge base for the others. `base` is now always the merge base.
  - `weakening::bounce` groups the caught paths by base. The new `contract::deleted_test_files_message` emits one `git checkout <base7> -- …` per base, joined by `&&`. A single base's text is unchanged.
  - Because the map is per file, only a caught deletion's base is ever used, which settles the `red_deleted` point; that flag is gone.
  - Tests:
    - `each_deleted_test_file_has_its_own_restore_base` (real git) failed before the fix with an empty map.
    - `each_deleted_test_restores_from_its_own_base` (engine) failed before the fix with a single `git checkout` for both files.
  - Fix round 2's `a_deleted_writers_test_is_restored_from_red` is folded into the first.
- **(c): a run-head change on a writer's path is not the implementer's.**
  - A writer's path is left out of the `red..head` read when the head holds it exactly as the run head does (`git diff --name-only <run_head>..<head>`).
  - This applies only when the run head also changed that path since `start`. Without that bound, an implementer that reverted the writer's change to a shared test would be hidden whenever the run head never touched the file.
  - Tests:
    - `a_run_head_change_on_a_writers_path_is_not_the_implementers` (real git) failed before the fix: the `tests/old.rs` loss was misattributed.
    - `reverting_the_writers_change_to_a_shared_test_is_caught` pins the bound. It fails with the bare equality rule.
- **(d): the writer's own claim.**
  - The writer's claim already read its signals as any worker's claim does. `weakening::spec` sets `red` only in the implementing phase, so `verify_done` takes `done_signals(run_head, head)`, which is the merge-base read over `start..red`.
  - `a_writers_claim_catches_its_weakening_of_an_existing_test` (real git) is therefore a pinning test: it passed before this round.
  - A deleted test bounces at the writer's own done gate, and an assertion loss is kept on the task.
  - Gap for the coordinator: decision 42 has the implementer's accepted claim replace the task's signals. A writer's non-deletion signals on an existing test therefore never reach the review. Carrying them over would change decision 42, so this was not done.
- **(e), NB-3.** The `tiers/spec.rs` docs for `ClaimSignals.base` and `SignalsSpec.red` now describe this behaviour.

  | `git/signals_pair.rs` | 119 (new) |

**Fix round 4 (2026-10-04), rulings T16-9 and T16-10, and re-review 3's NI-2, NI-3 and the order item.** Ruling T16-10 supersedes fix round 3's equality rule (c).

- **T16-9 (1): the reviewer sees the writer's signals.**
  - At the red check's hand-over, `pair::on_red_checked` moves the writer's kept claim signals into the new `Pair.writer_signals` and `Pair.writer_signals_more` (both `#[serde(default)]`).
  - `weakening::keep` appends them to the implementer's claim signals and adds both overflow counts. Decision 42's replacement therefore no longer discards them.
  - The reviewer block and `signal_infos` mark them by appending ` (test writer)` to their lines. `by_writer` identifies them: the trailing `writer_signals` of the task's list, while the pair is implementing. `check_review` requires their `W` ids to be answered like any other.
  - Test: `the_reviewer_sees_the_writers_signals_too`. Before the fix, the task's signals were only the implementer's.
- **T16-9 (2): the ARG_MAX fallback.**
  - Above `PATHSPEC_MAX` (256) writer paths, each pair read (own, `start..run_head`, merge-tree diff, `red..head`) runs over every path and is filtered afterwards. Below it, pathspecs are used.
  - The claim records the path count in the new `ClaimSignals.unlimited` (`#[serde(default)]`). `done_checked` then logs one run-log line through `pair::log_unlimited`: `task <id>'s test writer changed <n> paths, over the pathspec limit of 256; its signals were read without one and filtered`.
  - Tests:
    - `over_256_writer_paths_are_read_without_a_pathspec` (real git, 258 paths).
    - `an_unlimited_writer_path_read_is_logged` (engine).
- **T16-10: the clean 3-way merge replaces (c)'s equality rule.**
  - A writer path that the run head changed since `start` is dropped from the `red..head` read only when two things hold:
    - `git merge-tree --write-tree --merge-base=<start> --name-only --no-messages -z <red> <run_head>` reports no conflict on that path;
    - the head's blob equals the merged tree's there, checked with `git diff --name-only <tree>..<head>`.
  - A conflict, a difference, an output larger than was kept, or no usable answer keeps the path.
  - Version facts:
    - `merge-tree --write-tree` arrived in git 2.38, which is the run engine's minimum (`tests/run_git.rs`).
    - `--merge-base` with `--write-tree` arrived in 2.40.
    - `merge-file --object-id` arrived in 2.43.
    - `merge-tree` is chosen because it is the older of the two and needs no blob files. On 2.38 and 2.39 the option is unknown, so `merge-tree` exits 129 with nothing on stdout and the path is kept.
    - Checked on git 2.50.1.
  - `merge-tree` writes objects only, as in decision 36's candidate merge.
  - Tests:
    - `a_theirs_resolution_dropping_the_writers_test_is_caught`: the reviewer's reproduction. The writer adds `fn extra`, the run head adds `fn other`, and a refresh conflict is resolved with `--theirs`. Before the fix it raised no signal.
    - `without_merge_base_the_writers_path_is_kept`: a wrapper git refuses `--merge-base`.
    - `a_run_head_change_on_a_writers_path_is_not_the_implementers` still passes.
- **NI-2.** `worktree::run_git_capturing`, which every daemon git call goes through, now also removes `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS`, `GIT_GLOB_PATHSPECS`, `GIT_NOGLOB_PATHSPECS` and `GIT_ICASE_PATHSPECS`.
  - Test: the new one-test binary `tests/run_git_pathspec_env.rs`. It sets `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1`, which git refuses in combination with any other mode. It is a single test in its own binary, as `worktree_env.rs` is, because no lock covers a concurrent process spawn. It failed before the fix: git matched nothing.
  - Adding the four variables to AGENTS.md rule 11's list is left to the docs pass, as the ruling says.
- **NI-3.** With more than one base, the fallback restore text is `Restore each deleted test file from its commit (<path> from <sha7>; …), then commit,`. A single base's text is unchanged.
  - Test: `the_fallback_restore_text_names_each_files_base`.
- **Order.** Within each rank (deleted, `DiffTooLarge`, the rest), the writer's paths' signals come before the implementer's own.
  - Test: `the_writers_test_loss_is_listed_ahead_of_the_implementers_own`, with 21 of the implementer's own losses. It fails with the old order.

### Task M9.5.17a (2026-10-04): the race in the reducer I: dispatch, lanes, gates per lane, elimination

- **Move-only split first.** `dispatch_launch.rs` had no budget left, so `launch_worker`, `launch_implementer`, `launch_fresh` and `launch` moved to a new `engine/dispatch_session.rs` in their own commit (`c76616ef`; 2970 test names before and after, identical). `dispatch_launch.rs` is 174 lines after 17a, `dispatch_session.rs` 173.
- **The lane view** (`engine/race_view.rs`, new). Rather than teach every gate module about lanes, each lane's events and scheduler passes run with the racing task *being* that lane: `race_view::in_lane` swaps the task's private fields with the lane's (`start_commit`, `head`, `done`, `failures`, `bounces`, `stalls`, `budget_exceeded`, the spend, `route`, `review_route`, and a new `Lane.gates: LaneGates` holding `rung`, `gate_op`, `claim`, `review_misses`, `pending_failure`, the claim's signals and refusals, `fresh_session`, `ready_from`, `worktree_live`, the clock and the block), maps `LaneState` to a `TaskState` (an `Out` or `Lost` lane shows as `Cancelled`, which every pass leaves alone), sets `worktree`/`branch` to the lane's checkout and branch, partitions rounds and the proof, check and review records by `lane`, parks the other lane's pending ops and outbox messages, and renames the lane's message addresses (`t1.a`, `t1.a.review`) to the task's inside the view. `Task.lane_view` (`#[serde(skip)]`) marks a view. On leaving, new rounds, records, ops and messages are tagged with the lane, parked items return in place, the lane's spend delta is added to the task's total, history lines written in the view are prefixed `racer <x>: `, and the view's state decides the lane: `MergeQueue` means it passed its last gate, `Blocked` means `Out` with the block's text; any other state is the lane's own. So the existing claim, proof, check or tier 1, weakening signals, review and ladder code serve each lane unchanged (ruling RR-3), and every op a lane sends names its checkout (`t1.b`, `t1.b.proof`, `t1.b.review`) and branch (ruling T1-3). `LaneGates` and `Race.crowned` are `serde(default)` and skipped while idle, so a persisted run without a race reads and writes as before.
- **Routing.** `OpDone` answers in `pending.lane`'s view (a `Decide` in the view of the lane whose `pending_failure` named its decider; a `CrownRacer` outside any view, since the crown is the task's); a `Signal` or a tool call in the view of its window's round's lane (`race::lane_of_window`); a `Delivered` in the view of its address's lane. The whole idempotent passes a lane needs (`restore::relaunch`, `signals::watch`, `ladder::recover_sessionless`, `ladder::start_fresh_sessions`, `gates::start_gates`, `review::watch`, `launch_ready`, `review::dispatch_reviewers`, plus `clock::watch_open_turns`, `outbox::deliver` and `clock::sync`) run once more inside each live lane's view (`race::each_lane`, `dispatch::lane_passes`); every other task looks the same in a view as outside it, so they see nothing new. In a view, `gates::enter(MergeQueue)` does not join `run.merge_queue`, `dispatch::block` only makes the view `Blocked`, and `done::task_blocked` without a kind skips the decider.
- **Dispatch (decision 18).** `race::start` returns `Single` (with a note), `Race(peer)` or `Wait`. The notes, each pushed once to `task.notes`, written to the task's history and logged as `task <id>: <note>`: `race skipped: not on the critical path at dispatch`, `race skipped: max_writers is 1`, `race skipped: no installed <runtime> model at strength <strength> for the second racer`, `race skipped: no second writer slot within <n> s`. A race starts when two writer slots are free and both runtimes have room under their caps (`concurrency::cap`); otherwise it waits from `Task.race_wait_since` for `limits.race_slot_wait_secs` (new in `RunLimits`, from the tuning table's `race_slot_wait_secs`, skipped at zero), holding the head of the line (`dispatch_writers` breaks on a `Wait` at the head of the line, and passes over one further back). `dispatch_race` sets the task `working`, writes `dispatched as a race: racer a on <runtime>, racer b on <runtime>`, builds both lanes (lane a on the task's route, lane b on `peer_route`, each with its `review_route`), and prepares each lane's checkout in its view. A racer's spec is `role_launch_patterns::racer_spec`: exactly a worker's (`worker_spec` with the lane's checkout and route), its MCP target and `RunRef` saying `Racer` and the lane. Windows are `<h4>/t1.aw1`, `<h4>/t1.bw2`, reviewers `<h4>/t1.ar1` (`race::stem`); session numbers are the task's, so lane b's racer is session 2. `prewarm` skips a racing task.
- **Writer slots** (ruling RR-9, the addendum). `writer_slots` counts one slot per live lane only while the race has no winner, the task's single slot once it is crowned or adopted, and none for a finished (cancelled) task whatever its lanes say. `readers_busy` adds one reader per lane in review (`race::lane_readers`), an approximation by lane state: a lane's reviewer is counted by its lane being in `Review`, as a task's is. The driver's test slots need no change: they schedule `Proof`, `Check` and `Tier` by op kind, whichever lane sent them.
- **Elimination (decision 20).** Rungs 2 and 3 in a view call `race::lane_out`: the lane's sessions stopped (`kill_worker`, the existing `KillWindow` path; ruling RR-2, no new kill code), queued messages dropped, the rung kept on the lane, then blocked (`Human` for rung 2, `MisSized` for rung 3, as the lane's block) so the view leaves as `Out` with the gate's text. Nothing of the task's own changes: no escalation, no fresh session, no size raised. `task_blocked` in a lane does the same with the racer's reason. `race::eliminate` stops the lane's remaining live rounds (a reviewer through `review::give_up`), sets the lane `Out` with its reason and `kill_sent_at`, answers a pending claim with `racer <x> of task <id> is out: <reason>. Stop now.`, drops its outbox messages and logs `race <task>: racer <x> out: <reason>`. Later tool calls from that lane get the same refusal; once a lane has won, the other lane's get `the race for task <id> is over: racer <w> won. Stop now.` **Interim until task M9.5.17b:** when both lanes are out the task is blocked on its environment with `both racers are out (racer a: <reason>; racer b: <reason>)`; adoption, waiting for a loser's exit, salvage and `keep_path` are 17b's, and a loser keeps running after the other lane wins until then.
- **The crown (decision 21, rulings T1-2, T1-3).** The first lane to leave its view in `MergeQueue` is marked `Won`, `race.winner` set, `race <task>: racer <x> won` logged, and only then is `CrownRacer` sent, tagged with the lane (the addendum's watch item; a later lane passing writes `racer <x> passed after racer <w> won` and nothing else). `Crowned` makes the task the lane's (`race_view::become_lane` copies the lane's gate state, keeping the task's whole spend), sets `race.crowned`, `task.worktree = run.task_path(checkout)` together with `task.branch` (the task branch the crown created) and `task.head`, renames the lane's queued messages to the task's addresses, enters the merge queue and writes `racer <x> crowned at <sha7>; next: merge queue`. From then on every `task.worktree` site follows the lane (hand-back and merge carry `t1.b` and the lane's head). `RefMoved` halts the run (`merge::halt`); `Failed` blocks the task on its environment with `could not crown the race's winner: <message>`. Between `Won` and `Crowned` the winner's view is inert: its racer's tool calls are refused with `racer <x> of task <id> won the race; wait for the engine's next message`, so nothing imports through the won lane's pin. `race::crown_pass` re-sends `CrownRacer` for a winner neither crowned nor in flight, which is how a resume after `RefMoved` crowns again (the crown is a compare-and-swap). The minimal crown is in 17a so a race can finish; the loser's stop and adoption are 17b's.
- **Salvage refs.** A lane's `RemoveWorktree` result is answered in its view, where `dispatch::removed` pushes the ref onto `task.salvage_refs` (not swapped), so `merge::next_salvage_seq` never reuses a number (pinned by `a_lanes_salvage_ref_is_the_tasks`).
- **Restore.** `restore.rs::lost` destructures `PendingOp` exhaustively again (the addendum). `relaunch` outside a view skips lane rounds (their lane's view relaunches them) and treats a `Racer` as a writer. Restarting and restoring a race across a daemon restart (re-entering lanes, the restart pin) is 17b's; `Task.lane_view` is never persisted.
- **The `AgentRole::Worker` audit** (decision 23). M9.5.1's 69 references were down to 25 non-test lines at 17a's base, task 16 having moved most behind `model::writes_task(role)`. 17a adds `Racer` to `writes_task` and a new `model::writes(task, round)`: a racer counts as the task's writer only in its lane's view or once its lane is crowned (`Task::crowned_lane`), so outside a view a racing task has no single worker. Through `writes`/`worker_round` now: `ladder::{worker_round, kill_worker, supersede, fresh_due}`, `ladder_budget::total_spend`, `clock::{restart, epoch_spend}`, `signals.rs` (stream events, the turn end, the exit), `outbox.rs` (queue, target, delivery failures), `dispatch.rs::window_done`, `edits_state::has_live_worker`, `actions/effects.rs::has_worker`, `merge.rs` (writers retired at the merge). Through `writes_task` (role only, now with `Racer`): `early.rs` (held tool calls, by role), `restore.rs::relaunch`, `snapshot.rs:179` (shown as `worker`), `orch/tools.rs:84` (a racer may call `task_note`), `driver/orch.rs:193` (the read tools of a `writes_task` role go the engine's way, to `parse_call`'s refusal; task 16's fix round 1, minor m5), `done.rs`/`worker_messages.rs` (the caller's role must be its round's). `review.rs:165` finds the author as the latest `Worker` or `Racer` (a pair's implementer is a `Worker`). Left worker-only, with the reason: `role_launch.rs:216,243` (the worker's own spec; `racer_spec` overrides both to `Racer`), `history.rs:190,192` (`worker_usage`, tool calls; tasks M9.5.20 and 21 own history), `snapshot.rs:304` and `snapshot_detail.rs:43` (the current session's spend and summary; tasks M9.5.20 and 21), `routing.rs:320` (a racer's routing decision is recorded as the worker's, one per session with the lane's route; history's), `driver/ops.rs:135` (`worker_git_dirs`, assigned to task M9.5.18's addendum by task 15's note; a sandboxed racer keeps its unresolved roots until then), `worker_messages.rs:301` (parses `task_note` arguments, role-independent), `pair.rs:64` (the implementer), `outbox.rs:242,244` (the address kinds: a racer is addressed as its task's worker, inside its view), `orch/roles.rs:60` (a label).
- **Tests.** Part 1 in `engine/tests/race.rs` (8), parts 2 and 3 in `engine/tests/race_lanes.rs` (14), plus `race_pair_plan.rs`'s test renamed `race_tasks_race_and_paired_tasks_start_their_test_writer` (it now asserts `RACE_DISPATCH` is open and sees `t1.aw1`/`t1.bw2` racers beside `t2`'s test writer). Beyond the brief's list: `each_lane_runs_tier_1_and_the_signals_on_a_tiered_profile`, `an_adopted_lanes_crown_moves_the_worktree_too`, `the_crown_is_sent_only_after_the_lane_is_won`, `a_moved_task_branch_halts_and_nothing_imports_before_the_crown`, `a_lanes_salvage_ref_is_the_tasks`, `a_race_whose_peer_is_not_installed_runs_single`, `a_race_that_ended_counts_as_its_task` (the addendum's items). The tests were written after the core implementation, not before; their RED was taken by closing `RACE_DISPATCH` again (23 of 25 race tests fail) and by reverting single changes (the finished-task slot rule). Writing them found one defect: a `CrownRacer` result was answered inside the winner's view, so the crown's `task.worktree` was undone on leaving it.
- **Sizes.** New: `race.rs` 518, `race_view.rs` 349, `dispatch_session.rs` 173; tests `race.rs` 398, `race_lanes.rs` 588. Growth: `ladder.rs` +7 (452 → 459, within +16; `lane_out` lives in `race.rs`), `signals.rs` +11 (581), `restore.rs` +12 (541), `dispatch.rs` +30 (386), `done.rs` +17 (357), `results.rs` +34 (161). No file under `run/engine/` exceeds 600 lines. The brief's file list named `done_checked.rs`, `review_session.rs`, `worker_messages.rs` and the `run/slots/` callers; the view made changes there unnecessary.
- **Fix round 1 (2026-10-04; rulings T17a-1, T17a-2 and the review's minors).**
  - *T17a-1, the race decision latched.* `Task.race_decision: Option<RaceDecision>` (new, `#[serde(default)]`, skipped while `None`; `model_tuning_tests::NEW_KEYS` counts it) is set the first time `race::start` decides: `Race` when the race starts, `Single { reason }` (the note) on any fallback. Once set, `start` returns `Single` silently, so the note, the history line and the run-log line `task <id>: <note>` are written once, and a task skipped as off the critical path stays single for that dispatch even if it leads the path later. `race_wait_since` is set once and kept across passes until the wait gives up; after that the latch keeps the task from waiting again, so the head of the line is held once. The latch is cleared only where a task goes back to be dispatched again, `requests.rs`'s rung-2 retry of a task with no start commit (it becomes `queued`). Tests (`engine/tests/race_slots.rs`): `a_single_racer_held_by_its_runtimes_cap_logs_its_line_once` and `the_slot_wait_gives_up_once`.
  - *T17a-2, lane readers by the `holds_reader` rule.* `race::lane_holds_reader` counts a lane in `review` only with a live reviewer round of that lane, a resumable one that owes its verdict, or a `PrepareReview` of that lane in flight. Inside a lane's view the other lanes' rounds and ops wait outside, so `race_view::enter` counts them first into `Task.parked_readers` (`#[serde(skip)]`, zero outside a view), which `lane_readers` reads for a task in a view. Test: `both_lanes_wait_for_one_reader_slot_and_are_reviewed_in_turn` (`max_readers = 1`, a research task holding the slot; before the fix, neither lane ever got its `PrepareReview`).
  - *m5.* `writer_slots` counts a race with a winner on the winning lane's runtime (from `Won`, before the crown swaps the route in; and for an adopted lane b). Test: `a_won_or_adopted_lane_b_holds_its_slot_on_its_runtime`.
  - *m2.* `a_moved_task_branch_halts_and_nothing_imports_before_the_crown` now restarts the daemon (`Restore`) and resumes through the real request (`Resume` with `--rebaseline`, which a non-retryable halt needs); across the restart, the resume and the next tick there is one `CrownRacer`, no claim, count, hand-back, diff or merge op, and no other op naming lane b's checkout. It passed on the existing code; a mutant without `crown_pass` fails it.
  - *m3, m4.* The crown tests moved to `engine/tests/race_crown.rs` (`race_lanes.rs` would have passed 600 lines). `the_crown_moves_the_tasks_worktree_and_branch` follows the hand-back to the winner's next claim, checked in `t1.b`. A crowned task never prepares a worktree again (its checkout exists; a retry starts a fresh session in it), so the brief's "next `PrepareWorktree`" is checked as the fresh session's `DiffSoFar` and `CreateWindow` in `t1.b`. Part 3's constructed post-crown half is replaced by `worker_checks_see_the_crowned_lane`, through the reducer: lane b passes and is crowned while lane a's racer still runs; `worker_round`, `has_live_worker`, `total_spend` and rung 4's `clock::epoch_spend` see lane b's racer alone; a retry stops lane b's racer only, and the fresh session waits for its exit alone. A mutant counting every racer as a writer fails it.
  - *m7.* A lane out at rung 2 or 3 keeps no block reason: `race::lane_out` no longer goes through `dispatch::block` (no `blocked (human)` history line), and `race_view::leave` keeps `Lane.gates.block` only for a lane out below rung 2 (a `task_blocked`). The rung says why.
  - *m9* is recorded under Risks and gotchas (17). *m1* and *m8* moved to task M9.5.17b's addendum; *m6* was task 16's fix round (T16-4).
  - Sizes: `race.rs` 549, `race_view.rs` 358, `schedule.rs` 382, `requests.rs` 438 (+2); tests `race.rs` 398, `race_lanes.rs` 394, `race_crown.rs` 312, `race_slots.rs` 203.
- **Fix round 2 (2026-10-04; rulings T17a-3, T17a-4, T17a-5 and N2).**
  - *T17a-3, T17a-4: one queued stint per latch.* `phases::set_state` clears `race_decision` and `race_wait_since` whenever a task moves to `pending`. That covers `requeue_waiting` (`edits.rs`, a queued task that gained an unfinished dependency), the dep-cancelled release (`edits_orch.rs`), and `dispatch::requeue` (a queued task whose stage is no longer ready). Only a task that has not started can be `pending`. `amend_task` also clears both on a not-started task when it amends `race`, `pair`, `route`, `stage` or `deps`, even if the task stays queued. The rung-2 retry back to `queued` (`requests.rs`) already cleared it. The engine has no separate reset of a task. Tuning or roster changes while the task stays queued keep the latch. Tests (`race_slots.rs`): `an_amended_race_clears_the_latch` (a queued racing task decided single and held by Claude's cap is amended to `race = false`, then starts one ordinary worker, with the first stint's line only) and `a_task_back_to_pending_decides_again` (`add_dep` sends it to `pending` through `requeue_waiting`; once queued again it decides afresh).
  - *T17a-5 (N1).* `writer_slots` uses the winning lane's runtime only while the race is not crowned. Once crowned (adoption included, since `on_crowned` swaps the lane's route in through `become_lane`), the slot is on `task.route.runtime`, so a later rung 2 or retry that changes runtime moves the slot with it. The constructed adopted case is replaced by real crowns through the reducer: `a_crowned_race_counts_its_slot_on_the_tasks_route` and `an_adopted_lane_b_counts_its_slot_on_the_tasks_route`. Each hands the crowned task back to `working`, sees the slot on Codex, changes the route to Claude and sees it follow. `a_won_lane_b_holds_its_slot_on_its_runtime_until_the_crown` keeps the window before the crown.
  - *N2.* `a_moved_task_branch_halts_and_nothing_imports_before_the_crown` asserts that the re-sent crown names `t1.b` and lane b's head.

### Task M9.5.17b (2026-10-04): the race in the reducer II: the crown, the loser, adoption, messages and actions

Decisions 20–23 and rulings RR-1, RR-2 and RR-4 as written, with the controller's addendum (task 17a's m1, m8 and re-review items (a)–(d)) and these readings:

- **Code layout.** A move-only commit first (`bc8851ee`): the end of a race (`on_lane_passed`, `crown`, `crown_pass`, `eliminate`, `crown_done`, `on_crowned`) moved unchanged from `race.rs` to the new `engine/race_end.rs`. The stopped lane's salvage is a second new file, `engine/race_salvage.rs` (not in the file list), so each stays focused. `engine/batch.rs` (not in the file list) takes the cancel edit's half.
- **Winning (m1).** The first lane past its last gate is `Won`, and every other live lane is `Lost` at once, in the same step: its racer and its live reviewer are stopped through the existing `KillWindow` path (a reviewer through `review::give_up`), a claim it waits on is answered `the race for task <id> is over: racer <w> won. Stop now.`, its queued messages are dropped, and the run log says `race <task>: racer <x> lost: racer <w> won`. A lost lane is never reviewed again (`a_lane_that_passes_after_the_win_gets_no_new_review_round`). `race::refusal` now answers an `Out` lane with its own reason even after the other lane has won; a `Lost` one gets the winner's sentence.
- **The stop and the salvage (RR-2; `race_salvage.rs`).** `stop_lane` sets `Lane.kill_sent_at` to the stop's time for every stopped lane (with or without a live racer), so the wait has a start. `race_salvage::pass` runs every scheduler pass, whatever the task's or run's state (beside `remove_cancelled_worktrees`, so a cancelled task's lanes too). For a lane `Out` or `Lost`, not yet removed or kept, whose checkout exists (`gates.worktree_live`) and that has no op in flight (`PendingOp.lane`), it emits `RemoveWorktree { keep_head: true, clear_locks: true, keep_path: false }` once no racer round of the lane is live (the racer's `ProcessExited` ended it, or it had ended), else, at `kill_sent_at + LANE_EXIT_WAIT_SECS` (`process::KILL_GRACE` + 30 = 33 s), `RemoveWorktree { keep_head: true, keep_path: true, clear_locks: false }` with `Lane.kept` and the log line `race <task>: kept <x> checkout: its racer did not exit`. The lane's path is unwatched first. The removal's result is answered outside any view (`results.rs`, `race_salvage::owns`): `salvage_ref` and `cleared_locks` land on the lane and the ref on `task.salvage_refs`; each cleared lock is a history line `racer <x>: removed a stale <file> left by the stopped racer`. A failed removal marks the lane kept (its checkout goes with the run's at accept or discard) rather than retrying every pass. **Salvage numbering:** the ref is reserved on the lane when the removal is sent, and `merge::next_salvage_seq` counts the lanes' reserved refs, so two lanes salvaged together, or a lane's salvage in flight while the crowned task merges, never share a number.
- **Adoption (decision 20).** `race_end::eliminate` adopts instead of eliminating when no other lane is live and there is no winner. The adopted lane is `Adopted`, `race.winner` and `race.adopted` are set, the log says `race <task>: racer <x> adopted: <reason>`, and `CrownRacer { adopt: true }` is sent at the lane's last imported head, else its start commit. Its session is not stopped by the adoption (a racer that asked a question keeps waiting for its answer; rung 2 and 3 already stopped theirs in the lane's view). After `Crowned` the task takes the lane (as for a win) and, instead of the merge queue, the ladder applies the action that took the lane out (`after_adoption`): the lane's `gates.rung` 3 → `ladder::rung3`, 2 → `ladder::rung2` (a fresh session in the adopted checkout), else the lane's own block (`blocked(question)` with the racer's text, `blocked(environment)`), else `blocked(environment)` with the reason. A last lane with neither a head nor a start commit (its checkout was never prepared) is not crowned: it is `Out`, and the task is `blocked(environment)` with that lane's reason; this replaces 17a's `both racers are out (…)` stopgap.
- **m8.** The crown's head is `crown_head`: the lane's head, or for an adopted lane its start commit, never empty. A winner with neither is not crowned; the task is `blocked(environment)`, `could not crown the race's winner: racer <x> has no commit to crown`.
- **A failed crown (beyond the brief).** 17a's `crown_pass` re-sent the crown for every winner not crowned whose task was not finished, so a crown that failed (`blocked(environment)`) was sent again on the next pass, in a loop. `crown_pass` now skips a blocked task, and `run retry` of a task whose winner waits for its crown (`race_end::retry_crown`) sets it working, so the next pass sends the crown again (`task <id> retried: its race's winner is crowned again`). Test `a_failed_crown_waits_for_a_retry` (`race_crown.rs`; a mutant without the skip fails it). `crown_done` ignores a result for a task cancelled meanwhile.
- **Cancel (decision 23).** `race_end::cancel` stops every lane but a crowned one (live lanes and a winner waiting for its crown), each `Out` with reason `cancelled`; `race_salvage::pass` salvages both once their racers exit. It is called from `complete::cancel_now` and from the `cancel_task` edit's `CancelLive` consequence (`batch.rs`), which kills a cancelled task's sessions directly.
- **Messages (RR-4).** `outbox::queue` fans a message to a racing task (not in a view, not crowned or ended) out to each live lane's address (`<task>.<x>`, its racer's window), or, between `Won` and the crown, to the winner's address, which the crown renames to the task's. So `message`, an amended brief (`EditConsequence::Deliver`), an answer and the restart's resume all reach every live lane. `worker_messages::queue` links the recorded message to the first copy.
- **Refresh (RR-4).** `LaneGates.refresh` (new, `#[serde(default)]`, skipped while `None`) is the lane's own refresh, swapped with `task.orch.refresh` in the lane's view (`race_view::exchange`). `refresh_pass` moves a racing task's due refresh to each live lane (history `refresh due for racer a and racer b`) and runs once more in each live lane's view, where the existing boundary rule hands the run head back into the lane's checkout. `refresh_refusal` also refuses while a live lane's refresh is due or in flight; a lost lane's refresh `HandBack` lost at a restart is due again (`restore.rs::lost`).
- **`stop_and_wait` (RR-4, review ruling I8).** `edits_orch::takes` refuses it for a racing task with the exact text, before the state rules; the action menu is unchanged (`actions_matrix.rs` passes as it is).
- **Retry and override (decision 23).** `Task::racing()` (new, `model_task.rs`): the race is neither crowned nor `ended`, and it has a winner waiting for its crown or a live lane. `rules::retry` refuses a racing task that is not blocked with `task <id> is racing: it has nothing to retry until one racer is left` (a blocked racing task is one whose crown failed, which retry crowns again); `rules::override_refusal` refuses any racing task with `task <id> is racing: there is no failed gate to override`. After adoption and the crown, the task is ordinary and both apply.
- **Notes (RR-4).** `WorkerNote.lane` (`#[serde(default)]`, skipped while `None`) is the view's lane; the digest's note entry gains `"lane": "lane <x>"` only when set, so `digest_fixture.json` is unchanged.
- **Restart.** `restore::resumed` also resumes each live lane in its view (`RESUME_WORKER` through the lane's address, a reviewer owing its verdict likewise). A winner waiting for its crown is not live, so after a `RefMoved` halt and a restart it is neither resumed nor messaged, and nothing imports through the pin task 15 gives it under the task branch: 17a's `a_moved_task_branch_halts_and_nothing_imports_before_the_crown` (restart, `Resume --rebaseline`, tick) pins it and passes unchanged. A stopped lane whose racer died with the old daemon is salvaged on the first pass (the restore ends every round). `driver/restore.rs` needed no change: `pin_worktrees` skips a removed lane's path.
- **Addendum (a).** `schedule::holds_reader` counts only the task's own reviewer rounds and `PrepareReview`s: in a view every one it shows, otherwise those of no lane or of the crowned lane (`own_lane`); `resumable_reviewer` likewise, matching the review record's lane. `race::lane_holds_reader` never counts the crowned lane, and counts a lane that left the race (`Out`, `Lost`) while its given-up reviewer has not exited (ruling T13-I2). Each reviewer is counted once, and a loser's reviewer no longer stands in for the crowned task's review.
- **Addendum (b).** `Race.ended` (new, `#[serde(default)]`, skipped while false): `requests::rung2`'s dispatch-again branch (`run retry` of a task with no start commit) ends a race with no winner. An ended race counts as an ordinary task in `writer_slots`, is not racing, and sends no message to its lanes; its lanes stay for their salvage and the run's clean-up. The single worker runs in the task's own checkout `t1`.
- **Addendum (c).** `edits_state::has_started` also counts a task with a race (`task.race.is_some()`: it was dispatched as one). `not_started`, which `add_dep`, `split` and the restricted-field amends use, is unchanged: a blocked race whose lanes were never prepared still takes those (only its race and pair, and its stage, are refused).
- **Addendum (d).** An amend of `size` clears the latch of a task that has not started. The amended task is decided again in the same pass, so the test sees a second `race skipped` line rather than an empty latch.
- **Tests.** `engine/tests/race_end.rs` (15) and `engine/tests/race_end_requests.rs` (9), plus `race_slots.rs::an_amended_size_clears_the_latch` and `race_crown.rs::a_failed_crown_waits_for_a_retry`. Written before the code: RED, once the unreviewed fixture was right (`review_small = false`), was 22 of 25 failing; three are pinning tests that passed before the change (`a_losers_tool_call_is_refused_with_the_winner`, `after_the_crown_the_task_is_an_ordinary_task`, `a_pass_after_a_moved_branch_crowns_nothing_and_a_resume_crowns_the_winner`); three were rewritten after RED (the resume test gives lane b's Codex racer a session id, the size test counts the re-decision's line, the reader test lets the winner's retired reviewer exit) and each fails with its fix reverted. Five 17a tests changed with the rules: `each_lane_runs_every_pre_merge_gate` (the other lane is `Lost`), `worker_checks_see_the_crowned_lane` (lane a's racer was killed once, at the win), `both_lanes_wait_for_one_reader_slot_and_are_reviewed_in_turn` (the first review asks for changes, since an approval now ends the other lane), and the two constructed adoptions, `an_adopted_lanes_crown_moves_the_worktree_too` and `an_adopted_lane_b_counts_its_slot_on_the_tasks_route`, which now carry the lane's question and see the task blocked on it (the second answers it, then checks the slot).
